### Concerning
# CHRIST'S TESTAMENTS,
#### Namely:
# Baptism
#### and the
# Supper.
### Written in two Books.

-> **The First: Concerning Holy Baptism**, how it is to be understood in its **foundation** <note>original: "Ground," a central term in Boehme's mysticism referring to the essential basis or source of reality</note>, and why a *Christian* should be **baptized**. <-

-> **The Second: Concerning the Holy Supper of the Lord** *Christ*, what it is, with its **benefits** and *effects*, and how the same may be worthily *participated* in. <-

-> **And how these are to be understood, according to both the Old and New Testaments.** <-

-> *Set forth from the true theosophical foundation <note>theosophical: relating to "theosophy," a system of mystical insight into the nature of God and the soul's relationship to the divine</note>, through the three principles of divine revelation, and presented to the children of God for the information of their understanding.* <-

-> *also known as the Teutonic Philosopher <note>original: "Teutonicus Philosophus"</note>.* <-

<meta>This is the beginning of the English translator's preface, introducing a work on the sacraments of Baptism and the Lord's Supper.</meta>

# The Translator’s Preface.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'T' featuring floral and vine motifs within a square border.</image-desc>

These two little books, titled together by the author as the *Book of the Two Testaments*—one concerning *Baptism* and the other concerning the *Supper*—handle those two grand mysteries more deeply and plainly than any book in existence since the time of the Apostles. They clearly satisfy doubts and put an end to the differing opinions concerning each of them, and so make all parties friends again: those who are called *Anabaptists* <note>Those who believe baptism should only be for adult believers</note> and those who are called *Paedobaptists* <note>Those who practice infant baptism</note>; those who baptize adults upon the confession of their *faith*, and those who baptize *infants* with *witnesses* <note>Godparents</note> to stand for them. 

Also, this treatise suggests enough that one may discern who is a fit *administrator* of *baptism* to those newly converted, or to the infant children of converted parents. It reconciles the differences of opinion about *Transubstantiation* <note>The belief that bread and wine become the actual body and blood of Christ</note>, *Consubstantiation* <note>The belief that the body and blood of Christ coexist with the bread and wine</note>, and the *participation of mere bread and wine*. It shows how to truly understand the *right* participation of the true flesh and blood of Christ under the forms of bread and wine. Furthermore, since there is a real participation to be had of the flesh and blood of Christ *without* bread and wine, it explains why the bread and wine were instituted to be celebrated and shared. So also, seeing that the baptism of the Holy...

<summary>The translator introduces two treatises on Baptism and the Lord's Supper, claiming they resolve long-standing theological disputes between different Christian factions regarding the nature and administration of the sacraments.</summary>
<keywords>Baptism, Lord's Supper, Anabaptists, Paedobaptists, Transubstantiation, Consubstantiation, Sacraments, Church Unity</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the translator's preface, specifically addressing the necessity of outward religious rituals and the definition of divine appointments.</meta>

# The Translator's Preface

The Holy Spirit has been present many times *without* water; nevertheless, it is explained why the baptism of *water* is *necessary* to be used, even for those who had previously received the Holy Spirit just as the Apostles did.

When all of this is understood, it will likewise appear what the foundation of the *preaching* of the *Word of God* is, as it is an *ordinance of God* <note>original: "Ordinance of God"; a decree or religious rite established by divine authority</note>. Furthermore, we shall truly perceive the *essence* of all ordinances as they are so called (which, in the literal meaning of the word, is nothing more than a thing ordained or appointed). Thus, it will be easy to find how far short men fall of rightly valuing the *true* ordinances, and how eager they are to accept things as such which are *not indeed* the ordinance of God.

The *preaching of the Word*, the *administration of baptism*, and the *participation of the Lord's Supper* are ordinances of God. The *communion of saints* or holy people (*two or three met in Christ's name*) are a true church or congregation of God according to His holy ordinance. The *magistrate*, ruler, or king, and all who are in authority, are the ordinance of God for the punishment of evildoers and for the encouragement of those who do well. *God's ministers*, *presbyters* <note>original: "Presbyters"; from the Greek 'presbyteros', meaning elders</note> or elders, old men or fathers in the experiential knowledge of Christ; *His bishops* or overseers, shepherds or pastors, or feeders of His flock; *His doctors* <note>original: "Doctours"; in the sense of learned scholars or theologians</note> or *teachers*, instructors in the ways of godliness; or *prophets* appointed to tell people of their sins; also *evangelists* or messengers of the glad tidings of salvation; disciples...

<summary>The translator argues that while the Holy Spirit can act independently, outward ordinances like baptism and preaching remain divine requirements. He defines the true nature of a church and the roles of various religious and civil authorities as established by God.</summary>
<keywords>Baptism, Holy Spirit, Ordinances, Preaching, Communion of Saints, Church Government, Magistrates, Presbyters, Bishops, Evangelists</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the translator's preface, discussing the nature of divine ordinances and the necessity of knowing God through love.</meta>

# The Translator's Preface

Or scholars in the school of Christ, such as are taught by God; and *Apostles*, or those who are sent by God and Christ through the Holy Spirit; these must necessarily be ordained and appointed by God. And so, the forgiveness of sins or *Absolution*; *Excommunication*, or casting out from the congregation of Christ and delivering to Satan; the *healing* of the sick by the elders' fervent prayer of faith; the *laying on of hands* by the presbytery <note>original: "Presbytery"; the body of elders or ministers</note> or eldership, by which the gift of the Holy Spirit was bestowed; and also *marrying* in the Lord—all of these are the ordinances of God. Indeed, every duty of man toward God, or of one person toward another mentioned in either the Old or New Testament, is clearly an ordinance of God.

But how anything is a *true* ordinance of God, and what it actually consists of, is hardly known and little considered as it should be. If it is to be certainly understood, there must be much exercise of our minds before we can go about examining it rightly. We must *know* God before we can know His ordinances, which are of His own appointment. If we do not know *who* He is, how can we know that we love Him, and that it is not something else which we love instead of Him? For it is said by the Apostle *John*: *"He who says he loves God and hates his brother is a liar, and the truth is not in him; for if a man does not love his brother whom he has seen, how can he love God whom he has not seen?"* And <margin>a 1 John 4:8</margin> *he who does not love does not know God, for God is love.* Furthermore, the same Apostle says that <margin>b 1 John 4:18</margin> *God is love, and he who dwells*

<summary>The author defines various Christian practices as divine ordinances and argues that true understanding of these rites requires a genuine knowledge of God rooted in brotherly love.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the translator's preface, focusing on the internal experience of divine love and the practical evidence of salvation through one's conduct.</meta>

# The Translator's Preface

*He who dwells in love, dwells in God and God in him:* and how can anyone know this excellent thing called Love—in <margin>1 John 3:14</margin> which whoever dwells <gloss>is passed from death to life</gloss>—if he does not know he has it within him? And if he does not know that, how can he either know God or love Him? This Love must be *within us* if we are to be the disciples of Christ. Indeed, there is nothing mentioned in the *whole* Bible concerning Heaven and Redemption that we must not possess *within us* while we are in this life; otherwise, we are not partakers of it. 

Yes, God has poured out His love *into* our hearts, which is *that word of Truth and life* that is *able to save our souls*, and does perfectly save <margin>Yield to it in obedience to its dictates.</margin> those who <gloss>receive it (that is, believe)</gloss>. This love is both poured out and received by very many souls who neither know what that *word* is, nor how they have *received* it. 

For example: when any man is *troubled* by his doing of *evil*, and is *sorry* for it, and wishes from his heart he had *not* done it; when he endeavors to keep a better watch over himself, resolves never to do the same again, and desires strength to be able to *resist* doing evil anymore; when he is careful lest evil suggestions in his mind should allure him and entice him to any evil again, and constantly *walks* thus circumspectly in *his whole life* to the *end* of his days—this man has the Love of God poured out in his heart and has received it. Otherwise, it could not bring forth such *fruits* of the Spirit. And *he who is so led by the Spirit is the child of God*, even though it may be that such a person does not know, if you ask him, what God’s Love is,

<summary>The author argues that salvation and the Love of God must be experienced internally during this life, manifesting as a sincere turning away from evil and a disciplined, moral life.</summary>

# The Translator's Preface

Even though he dwells within it; and the cause why there is so little progress in the *knowledge* of Christianity and the true *ordinances* <note>original: "Ordinances"; refers to established religious rites or divine decrees</note> of *God*, is because men think to learn those things by rote, and not by heart. Some are such enemies to themselves—which is deeply to be lamented—that if they read that the *Scripture says a thing*, they care *not what it means*, or how to understand it rightly. They fail to seek and find the *actual thing* being spoken of, so that they may be certain of the meaning of what they read in that inestimable book of God. 

Indeed, the Holy *Scriptures* have perfectly declared *all* the mysteries of God and his ordinances. *They* have been translated out of the original languages of *Hebrew* and *Greek* into the mother tongues of almost all nations, sufficiently to leave those people without excuse who are not obedient in their lives and conduct, so far as they *are able to know*. Therefore, it will be far easier for the *heathen* <note>original: "Heathen"; a term used historically for those who do not practice the Christian, Jewish, or Muslim faiths</note> to excuse themselves at the Last Day than for those who have read or heard the Scriptures and do not live accordingly. Though the heathen also shall be left without excuse, as the Scripture says: *God has left them without excuse, in that he does good, and gives them rain from heaven and fruitful seasons, filling their hearts with food and gladness.* <note>A paraphrase of Acts 14:17</note>

Furthermore, the Scriptures have had many laborious *expositions* <note>original: "Expositions"; detailed explanations or interpretations</note> of the words and renderings of both the original and translated copies, through large writings, commentaries, and glosses <note>original: "Glosses"; brief notations or interpretations of a text</note>, but

<summary>The translator argues that true Christian progress requires understanding the heart of Scripture rather than rote memorization, noting that while the Bible is widely translated, many fail to live by its teachings.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the translator's preface, emphasizing the necessity of personal spiritual experience over mere intellectual or traditional acceptance of religious truths.</meta>

# The Translator's Preface

The things which are undeniably meant by those words are not as well understood as they might be, if we would be more frequent and diligent in the practice of that which brings all wisdom and understanding: namely, *inward experience, and the examination or testing of spiritual things*. This is according to the words of the Apostle: *Test or examine all things, but hold fast to that which is good* <note>1 Thessalonians 5:21</note>. 

In outward matters, we are easily persuaded to "try before we trust," but concerning *inward* matters, we are for the most part ready to take them on trust. We never try or examine whether those things are actually so. We should be like the noble *Bereans* <note>A group of biblical Jews from Beroea praised in Acts 17:11 for their skepticism and diligence</note>, who were commended for *searching* or examining the Scriptures *to see whether those things were so or not*. This does not mean they were checking if the words were written down, but whether the *realities* mentioned were true in practice, for they already had experience of the things described in Scripture beforehand. 

But some fear that if we should be so inquisitive and pry into *mysteries* <note>term: mysteries; in this context, deep spiritual truths or divine secrets not immediately obvious to the senses</note>, we would run into the extreme of being *too wise*. This is impossible, except in our own conceit; for our Savior says: *Be as wise as serpents, but as innocent as doves* <note>Matthew 10:16</note>. Indeed, true wisdom is required to dispense the ordinances of God. This requires not only reading and forming mental concepts, but *living the life of Christ*. By this, we shall come to the true knowledge of Christ within us. And though he was in our parents before us, and in their offspring, and so has been in us from our very beginning and continually ever since, he is still *knocking and calling at the inward door of*

<summary>The translator argues that true spiritual understanding comes from personal "inward experience" and testing of truths rather than blind faith, citing the Bereans as a model for diligent inquiry.</summary>
<keywords>inward experience, spiritual examination, Bereans, mysteries, wisdom, Christ within, spiritual practice</keywords>

our hearts invisibly and without a form, and as the seed of God in the *embryo* <note>original: "Embrio"; referring to the earliest stage of spiritual life</note>, before our knowing and receiving of him. Yet after we have opened to *him*, and heard his call by yielding our hearts in obedience to it, he will take *a form in us*, and be born *in us*. We shall be regenerated <note>term: "regenerated" — spiritually reborn or made new</note> in him, and so we shall often feel him and see him. 

But after that, we must further attain *the stature of a man in Christ* <note>A reference to Ephesians 4:13, meaning spiritual maturity</note>, and be his disciples and scholars, and be taught by the Holy Spirit proceeding from him in us, before we can know and understand such mysteries as his ordinances <note>term: "ordinances" — divine decrees or religious rites</note> are. 

But if we did seek, *we should* find God and Christ in our hearts; for though they are in us, we find them *not* because we do not examine what and who they are. We are discouraged in our seeking, partly because many have sought in a way we have *accounted* as correct and yet have not found him. But the truth is, they have not sought in the *right place*. They look to find God anywhere but in their hearts; and yet, he who cannot find him there shall *never* be able to find him at all. But *there*, whoever seeks shall find him who is the fountain from which all things flow forth. 

The Scriptures flowed from him, and whatever could possibly have been written is *hidden in him*. The Prophets and Apostles wrote and spoke what they understood, but God was in their hearts and minds and filled them with the *understanding* of the Holy Spirit. Our Savior Jesus Christ did not have the Spirit by measure <note>meaning he had the Spirit infinitely or without limit</note>; for *in him dwelt the fullness of the Deity bodily* <note>original: "fullnesse of the Deity bodily"; a reference to Colossians 2:9</note>; and he

<summary>The author argues that spiritual maturity requires seeking God within one's own heart rather than through external means, as the heart is the source of all divine revelation and the dwelling place of the Holy Spirit.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the translator's preface, discussing the nature of divine understanding and the source of Christ's authority compared to the religious scholars of his time.</meta>

# The Translator's Preface

He grew in understanding until he had the anointing <note>original: "unction"; referring to the spiritual empowerment of Christ</note> above his fellows. From this understanding within him proceeded those sayings which led the people to confess that *he taught as one having power, and not as the Scribes and Pharisees*. Although those men were learned in the Scriptures, Christ said to them, "*You err, not knowing the Scriptures*"—meaning they did not know the significance of the things expressed in them—"*nor the Power of God*." 

He who does not understand the matter in his own mind which he intends to express cannot rightly speak or write about it. The Scriptures are writings by men full of Divine wisdom and understanding, but few other books are written by those who even believe that the *Apostles* had such a thorough knowledge of the divine mysteries they wrote about. 

There are some who seem to think that the writers of the Scriptures received their message by a type of revelation where a man or Angel dictated *words to them*, while they themselves had no more understanding of it than what their basic reason could grasp upon the bare *hearing of the words*. That is the only level of skill some in this current age believe is possible to attain regarding the mysteries of God. 

However, we are directed by the Apostle *James*—who well understood what he said through his own experience—that *if we lack understanding, we should ask it of God*. And though God is in everyone, and everyone lives, moves, and has their being in him, yet unless the Spirit of God moves and *teaches us in our understandings*, we do not truly know him.

<summary>The translator argues that true spiritual authority comes from internal divine understanding rather than rote learning, asserting that the biblical authors wrote from deep personal insight rather than blind dictation.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the translator's preface, focusing on the necessity of spiritual rebirth and internal experience over mere intellectual study.</meta>

# The Translator's Preface

Where the fruits of the Spirit are present in a person's heart, the Spirit of God has moved and taught them in their understanding. However, from that small measure of understanding, one is not able *immediately* <note>original: "presently"</note> to comprehend the *mysteries* of that Spirit and of those fruits—that is, where they originate, how they are conceived and brought forth, and how they grow. Nor can these things be known except by the *same* Spirit whose fruits they are. He who does not have the fruits of this Spirit—which are *holiness, peace, righteousness, and joy in the Holy Spirit*—does not enjoy the kingdom of God. Until we have sought and found peace and holiness, we shall not see God; and unless we are born again of water and the Holy Spirit, we cannot enter into <margin>* Luke 17:21</margin> the kingdom of Heaven, even though it is *within us*. 

Now, can anyone teach what it means to be born again, or what this water and Holy Spirit are, except for someone who *possesses them* and has been born again by them? I wish that men were diligent in desiring such *spiritual things*; then God, who dwells in their hearts, would feel and hear their desires. He would breathe forth His Holy Spirit within them and satisfy their desires with His gifts and graces. Then He would also *fill* their minds and understandings with the knowledge of *all* His mysteries. In this way, they would learn in the school of Christ through *divine experience* the true ordinances <note>original: "ordinances"; here meaning the established spiritual laws or decrees</note> of God. Then we would be better able to *build one another up in our most holy faith*, and by degrees—improving the measure of knowledge we already have—we would at length attain the *highest*

<summary>The translator argues that true spiritual understanding and the "mysteries" of God can only be grasped through the direct experience of being born again and the indwelling of the Holy Spirit. <keywords>Holy Spirit, spiritual rebirth, mysteries of God, divine experience, fruits of the Spirit, kingdom of heaven, holiness, inner light</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the translator's preface, emphasizing the practical application of spiritual knowledge and the pursuit of divine mysteries.</meta>

# The Translator's Preface

We lack nothing but the sincere *practice* of what we know; for by that means, we would not *quench* but stir up the gifts and graces of God’s Holy Spirit that are within us, until they multiply and flow forth with *all their gifts*. We suppose these are not attainable only because men have not endeavored to attain them. That is the reason we *possess the ordinances* <note>original: "Ordinances"; refers to established religious rites or ceremonies like baptism and the Lord's Supper</note> not in power, but in great weakness, lacking those gifts which would enable men to dispense the ordinances of God. 

It is a great gift to have our "calling and election sure" <note>A reference to 2 Peter 1:10</note> in the sense that the Apostle *Paul* meant it, according to the highest understanding of that *mystery*. What the Apostle himself knew must necessarily be attainable through labor and *endeavor*. Upon the *knowledge* of this mystery many other mysteries depend. It is for this purpose that the ordinances were *instituted*: as helps and means to bring us to the *assured certainty* of our salvation. 

We content ourselves with an imaginary, superficial *persuasion* without a knowing assurance. If we did endeavor, labor, search, seek, and try, we would soon *perceive* how it might be made infallibly sure; then we would just as assuredly understand the high excellence of *every ordinance of God*.

All the arts and trades in the world are *mysteries* <note>original: "Mysteries"; here meaning specialized crafts or bodies of knowledge requiring apprenticeship</note> and are not truly known except by those who have had *experience* in them. How then can there be a skillful dispenser of the mysteries of...

# The Translator's Preface

of God and his ordinances <note>original: "Ordinances"; here meaning God's decrees, laws, or established religious rites</note>; but rather by experiencing them. We are able to learn them *all* through experience, but without that, we know no more of them than a mere beast does. Even the devils, who far surpass mortal men in knowledge, still cannot know the mysteries of God because they cannot experience them; however, they *work* the mysteries of darkness, and therefore they know those. 

We are placed in this world to *work* the works of God, so that we may know what that good and acceptable will of God is. Therefore, we should *work out our salvation with fear and trembling* through obedience in well-doing. That obedience will lead us to feel and know the good Spirit of God, which will teach us the way and the method to find everything, if we search and inquire within that Spirit. 

Therefore, let us *endeavor* after that which is holy, and we shall feel what the God of holiness is. Then we shall clearly understand the Scriptures and the writings of all other men. We shall be able to *discover* what is true and what is false in the words and writings of any man, doing so with understanding and clear proof, to the *convincing* of all who hear us, regarding both divine and natural things.

From these instances that have been mentioned, it may be perceived that all the *ordinances of God* have proceeded from the *Holy Ghost*. Neither can anything be the ordinance of God where the Holy Ghost is not present in some measure or another. <meta>This page continues the translator's preface, focusing on the spiritual qualifications required for church leadership and the administration of religious rites.</meta>

# The Translator's Preface

God, any further than he is taught by the Spirit of God, and speaks what he understands by that same Spirit. None were fit to be made *Deacons* or *Elders* <note>original: "Presbyters"</note> unless they were full of the Holy Spirit; therefore, surely no one is able to appoint Deacons or Elders except by the power of the Holy Spirit within them. The same applies to all other ordinances <note>term: religious rituals or established rites, such as baptism or the Lord's Supper</note>. According to the measure of the Holy Spirit within us, such is the validity and *efficacy* <note>term: effectiveness or power to produce a desired result</note> of the ordinances to us. 

The outward performance of the things directed in the Scripture, when done by men of sincere hearts, is sufficient; these acts serve as warnings to us to forsake our evil ways. By observing this course, we may assuredly find true Christianity and, consequently, salvation. However, this is only the lowest degree of faith. It is to be wished that no one were so presumptuous as to claim for themselves that they are performing the true ordinances of God, unless they have the same Holy Spirit of God filling their hearts as those recorded in the Holy Scriptures who performed them correctly. 

When we are scarcely truly children of God, let us take care that we do not allow others to value us above what we are. Instead, let us inform them sincerely that we are not what they take us to be. We should not take it upon ourselves to be called God’s faithful servants, ministers of God, preachers of the Word, elders, teachers, pastors, overseers, messengers, and ambassadors of Christ, or apostles—those who are sent

<summary>The author argues that religious ordinances and church offices are only valid when performed by those truly filled with the Holy Spirit, warning against the presumption of claiming high spiritual titles without genuine divine inspiration.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the translator's preface, focusing on the distinction between true spiritual authority and those who merely claim the title of "Minister" or "Divine."</meta>

# The Translator's Preface

from God. Neither should any mention those texts of Scripture which speak of the true officers of Christ as if they were meant for and applicable to themselves. They urge these texts in sermons so that those listeners who lack the ability to discern the truth in things that differ are made confident to stiffly maintain that those Scriptures mean the people they call "Ministers," just as much as they meant the Apostles, Evangelists, and other faithful teachers in those days. They allege those Scriptures on their own behalf and call themselves "Divines" <note>original: "Divines"; a term for theologians or clergy</note>, whereas none of the Apostles were named so in all the Scripture except *John* the Divine, the beloved disciple, who was most sublime in vision and Revelation. 

When we truly examine ourselves according to the Scriptures, we shall find we ought rather to be esteemed enemies of God and of His Son Jesus Christ our Lord. But let us press forward in the ways of godliness, so that we may at length be strong men in Christ and enjoy His ordinances *in the power*, as it was and is in the spirits and souls of all the faithful holy ones. These are they who have true understanding and knowledge in the divine mysteries by experience; such were the prophets, the apostles, and the faithful in all ages.

No curious <note>original: "curious"; meaning highly skilled or expert</note> workman can make his servant expert in his work unless the servant tries and exercises himself, attaining the skill and readiness in his trade through practice and experience, so that he may be able not only to direct how to work

<summary>The author warns against ministers who falsely claim apostolic authority and titles like "Divine," arguing that true spiritual understanding comes only through personal experience and godliness.</summary>

# The Translator's Preface

work by hearsay, but to show his skill by his *own* doing of the same thing himself. The Apostles could do the same things which they taught others, or else they could not have *rightly* taught as they did.

Now, he that considers this book will find that the *author* knew and wrote experimentally <note>original: "Experimentally"; meaning through direct personal experience or spiritual practice rather than just theory</note>. If we *follow* his doctrine and experience, we may attain not only the understanding of his *writings*, but be able to speak and write *all that he understood*. Thus, we may disclose those things plainly and fully which he had neither the time nor the permission to write otherwise than *obscurely*. 

When we have arrived at this state, we shall *leave* that seeking after the most desirable worldly things, which pass away in a moment, and press after the high prize of the *calling* of Jesus Christ. Now, if by my means anyone shall be *stirred up* to strive, and so undoubtedly *attain* the blessed crown of eternal life, it will also be my *crown of rejoicing* in the world to come—though here I must acknowledge myself one of the unworthiest of the children of men.

->JOHN SPARROW<-

# A LETTER TO
# * Charles von Endern.

<margin>* Charles of Endern.</margin>

### Our salvation is in the Life of Jesus Christ in us.

Noble Sir, beloved in *Christ*: with the hearty desire for the Divine Light and the working power of the *Holy Being* <note>original: "Holy Ens"; a philosophical term for the essential substance or spiritual essence of God</note> in our Immanuel, I send over to you the *little book of Christ’s Testaments*, along with its preface. It should have been written only once in the first three sheets, for I intended to rewrite it all and had already begun with the first chapter. I am determined to bring this little book to the press in a more childlike <note>meaning: simple or easy to understand</note> manner, for the better understanding of the simple-hearted. 

However, because of the profound meaning, I have allowed the *one version to be written alongside the other <margin>* One by the other, one on one side, the other on the other, verse by verse.</margin>; seeing that you and other experienced lovers of truth understand this meaning well. In this way, those with sublime understanding may have that which is high, and the simple may have that which is lowly in its expression. Yet, there is one and the same understanding in them both, even though more simple words might be used in the lower sense. And so, I commit you to the continual, saving love of *Jesus Christ*. Dated at Görlitz, May 7th, in the year of Christ 1624.

->JACOB BOEHME.<- <note>original: "Jacob Behm"</note>

<summary>Jacob Boehme writes to Charles von Endern to deliver his manuscript on "Christ’s Testaments," explaining his decision to provide both a complex and a simplified version of the text to suit different readers.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Charles von Endern, Christ’s Testaments, Holy Ens, Görlitz, 17th-century theology, spiritual education</keywords>

# The Summary Contents of the Chapters in this first Book, concerning Holy Baptism.

### CHAPTER I.

How *Reason* is accustomed to view itself in a creaturely, image-like manner when it considers *Christ* and his *Testaments* <note>original: "Testaments"; here referring to the sacraments or covenants established by Christ</note>, and the origin of the conflict regarding Christ's Testaments, and how that conflict is an empty, unprofitable thing.

### CHAPTER II.

Concerning the Covenant of God after the Fall; what the Fall of Man is, and how God has bound himself in a Covenant with him again. What *Circumcision* in the Old Testament is, and what *Baptism* is in the New.

### CHAPTER III.

A brief and fundamental explanation of how Man is Baptized by the Holy Spirit through Christ's sufferings, Death, and Resurrection, in both body and soul.

### CHAPTER IV.

<margin>Or, to administer it.</margin>
Concerning the outward Water-Baptism by the hand of Man; who is worthy to so administer baptism, and which baptized Infant receives that Testament worthily; and how it fares with the *unworthy* hand <note>referring to an unworthy minister</note>, as well as with the unworthy Baptized Infant.

<summary>This table of contents outlines the first four chapters of the book, focusing on the spiritual nature of Christ's covenants, the transition from Old Testament circumcision to New Testament baptism, and the requirements for a "worthy" administration of the sacrament.</summary>
<keywords>Holy Baptism, Reason, Christ's Testaments, Covenant of God, Fall of Man, Circumcision, Holy Spirit, Water-Baptism, Sacramental Worthiness</keywords>

# The Preface to the Reader.

### SHOWING

## How the Testaments of Christ must be fundamentally understood, rightly participated in, and worthily fed upon.

### A Looking Glass <note>original: "Looking Glasse"; a mirror or model for self-reflection</note> for Teacher and Hearer.

->**I.**<-

Christian loving Reader, this little book concerning the Testaments <note>original: "Testaments"; here referring to the Sacraments, specifically Baptism and the Lord's Supper</note> of Christ ought to be well considered in this present time, when men strive only over opinions. Men have wholly and altogether departed from the *right understanding*, seeing as they try to maintain Christ’s Testaments with *disputing* and the *sword*.

2. There is no need for *disputing* about it; only an earnest, sincere, and penitent man who has the *faith* understands this testamentary <margin>a covenanting.</margin> institution and participation in the *power of Christ*. *But it is foolishness to the natural man, and cannot be apprehended* (**1 Corinthians 2:14**).

3. A quite different kind of earnest sincerity is required for true understanding, which is not grasped by human *reason*. It is rightly understood by no man unless the *Spirit of Christ* opens it to him in his *heart*.

4. There lies a firm seal before it which no reason or art can break open, except only that *slain Lamb of the House of Israel*, who has the *Key of David* (**Revelation 3:7; Chapter 5:5** <note>original citation "Chap. 3. 5." corrected to 5:5 based on the biblical reference to the Lamb opening the seals</note>).

5. To *reason* without the divine light, Christ’s Testaments are a closed book; but to the true children of Christ, they are an opened book. Christ’s Testaments are a seal of the firm and eternal covenant of God, by which God has received man into grace again after the horrible defection <note>original: "Defection"; the Fall of Man</note>. He has sealed man with the flowing forth of His love, by His blood and death, so that we <margin>or, might.</margin> should believe His word and promise. This is what He has revealed to us in the Holy Scripture concerning His Son—along with the redemption from sin—and what was previously declared by the prophets.

<summary>The author argues that Christ's sacraments cannot be understood through intellectual debate or force, but only through spiritual rebirth and the internal working of the Holy Spirit. He frames the Testaments as a divine covenant of grace established after the Fall of Man.</summary>
<keywords>Sacraments, Holy Spirit, Reason vs. Faith, Covenant of Grace, Key of David, Penitence, Religious Conflict</keywords>

--- Page 29 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's theological explanation of the Word of God and the nature of the New Covenant established through Christ.</meta>

# The Preface.

6. This *Word*, in the flowing forth of his Love, has come into our humanity and has assumed a human soul, as well as flesh and blood. He has established this Eternal Covenant with his blood and death. And so now he gives this *Testament* to his *faithful* ones 

<margin>c *Ausbeute*: Talent, or earnest money, or a pledge.</margin>
as a <note>original: "Pledge"</note> guarantee, and so makes them *branches* on the *vine* of his flesh and blood. In this way, they are embodied into him and made his true children, in whom HE himself will dwell with his flesh and blood through this testamentary guarantee. Thus, they may pray to the Father with joyful confidence in true childlike humility, in the name of his Son JESUS CHRIST, and so he will give them this grace (*John* 16:23).

7. This Testament he offers to us in two ways: First, through his *preached Word*, by which he stirs and opens the hearts of the hearers so that they enter into true repentance for their sins, and thus obtain for themselves this pledge of his Testaments.

<margin>d Or, Essential.</margin>
8. Secondly, through the *true* <note>original: "substantiall participation"</note> *essential partaking* of his flesh and blood by the mouth of faith with bread and wine. By this, he seals the faith with his blood and death and thereby opens the life's understanding of the *inward* divine hearing. Consequently, the poor fallen man (who was dead to divine hearing through sin) attains divine hearing again in his understanding. He is converted once more and enters into his first inheritance which he had in Paradise, giving up his will to God. God 

<margin>e Senses.</margin>
renews his heart, <note>original: "Thoughts"</note> senses, and mind through his <term>inspeaking</term> <note>The internal communication of God's Spirit to the human soul.</note> or inspiration. God continues and dwells in him with the pledge of this Testament, powerfully working in him through his faith, and generating him to be a new creature. This new creature walks in *heaven* with its *spirit* and is a true image of God. By this, the earthly, fleshly will is daily killed, and the newborn will daily goes to *heaven* (*John* 6:56; *Philippians* 3:20).

<margin>f Or, Senses.</margin>
9. This heaven is revealed in the inward ground of his life, in the Spirit of Christ within him. There, the good angelic <note>original: "thoughts"</note> senses climb up the true *Jacob's Ladder* (*Genesis* 28:12), and *Christ his Lord sits above at the top of it at the right hand of God*. Christ daily presents them—with his human and heavenly flesh and blood—before the wrath of God and the severe judgment, and is with the believer in all his need. He also encloses the believer's *prayers* within himself, and thereby presents them before death, hell, the devil, and the anger of God.

<margin>g Impregnated, or conceived.</margin>
10. A Christian should receive this publication of both his taught Word (as it is written in the *Bible*) and that which is <note>original: "received"</note> conceived in the sacramental participation, where he offers his Word in his flesh and blood.

<summary>The author explains how Christ's "Word" dwells in the believer through the sacraments and the preached Word, restoring the "divine hearing" lost at the Fall. This process transforms the believer into a "new creature" who spiritually ascends Jacob's Ladder to commune with God.</summary>
<keywords>Covenant, Testament, Eucharist, Jacob's Ladder, New Creature, Divine Hearing, Inspeaking, Grace</keywords>

# The Preface.

<margin>The Celebration.</margin>
receive, and find himself in this <note>original: "h" refers to the custom of the sacrament</note> custom, and unite and tie himself as a member with the *Congregation of Christ*; for in Christ we are all but one, as a tree <margin>Text: in.</margin> and its branches, *Galatians 3:28.*

11. It is not to be understood that this <term>Covenant</term> <note>A formal agreement or spiritual contract between God and humanity.</note> and <term>Testament</term> <note>The specific promise or legacy left by Christ, often referring to the Lord's Supper.</note> is given *only* by an *outward* hearing of the Word preached, and participation of bread and wine at the <margin>Or, Sacrament.</margin> Testament, as the present world so mistakenly believes in many hearts. No, it must be a right, sincere, and earnest effort with true *working* repentance, so that God—with the key of His *love*—unlocks and opens the *hearing* and the *right mouth* which shall receive this Testament. The poor soul must have a right hunger and thirst after it, and bring its *desire* to it through Christ’s sufferings, dying, death, and resurrection.

12. Otherwise, there is no true mouth for such participation. There must be a right, earnest, and sincere purpose that must put off the defiled garment and be *willing* to pass into a new life. It must be a matter of doing, doing, or it avails nothing.

13. This pledge belongs only to Christ’s children, who so hear and keep the Word in their *hearts* that it brings forth fruit. There must be great earnestness in both the teacher and the hearer; for if anyone wishes to handle and impart the Covenant of Christ, he must himself be capable of the Covenant and Testament. If the sheep must hear the voice of Christ out of the mouth of anyone and follow him, then also the *Spirit* and *power* of Christ must be in that man’s voice. *Otherwise, he is but a <term>hireling</term> <note>A person who works only for pay, lacking true spiritual commitment.</note>, and the sheep do not hear Christ’s voice from his mouth, but only the word of man, John 10.*

14. So also, in like manner, the hearer’s ear should be directed to God in true repentance, so that he also may hear the voice of Christ—not only with outward ears, but with the ears of *divine power*. In this way, the teacher’s and hearer’s power may strike together, so that the Spirit of Christ may work <margin>Text: Between them.</margin> together with them, and the heart may find the teacher’s power, so that good fruit may grow from it.

15. A teacher should not teach for the sake of wages only, but should know and well consider that he stands there in Christ’s stead, and that Christ will teach through him, if he be a *right* <margin>Or, Pastor.</margin> *shepherd*.

16. So also the hearers should incline their ear to *that*, and consider that they should there hear Christ’s voice and receive it with great earnestness. They should not think it is enough to go into the church and sit there for *an hour* to play the hypocrite and hear a *sermon*, only to remain afterward as they were before. No, such going to church and hearing is no service to God; it does not better them if, in the sermon or preaching, they have not heard *Christ teach in their hearts*.

<summary>The author argues that religious rituals like the Sacrament and hearing sermons are useless without sincere internal repentance and a "new life." He emphasizes that both the preacher and the listener must be spiritually aligned with Christ for the Word to be effective.</summary>

# The Preface.

Going to Church makes no one virtuous, unless he hears God's Word working effectively in his soul while in the Church.

17. So it is also with the **Sacraments** <note>The formal Christian rites, such as Baptism and Communion</note>; we should not think it is enough to **Confess** and go away, as if such a custom could take away sins without true **Repentance**, or that one may sin anew afterward. No, it is not so; whoever is washed and afterwards defiles himself with the same mire is then just as he was before.

18. Christ must **Absolve** <note>To formally release from guilt or sin</note> you in your soul through his sufferings and death, and "In-speak" or inspire his satisfaction into your soul; otherwise, it is of no value. The **Priest's Mouth** is only an **Outward** instrument and co-works in his Spirit; but if he is a **hireling** <note>A person working only for pay rather than spiritual devotion</note>, he cannot co-work. Nevertheless, the **Covenant** <note>A spiritual agreement or contract</note> of God in Christ Jesus works in the repentant heart and absolves it.

19. Loving Brethren, both teacher and hearer, who handle the Covenant of Christ: have a care what you do. There is great earnestness required, so that you are **not guilty of the death of Christ**. Consider diligently the great, severe earnestness of God—how HE instituted this Covenant through such great, hard pain and anguish, and through such great reproach and sufferings. It must surely be for a very great cause that this **Testament** <note>A will or formal decree; here referring to the New Covenant</note> was ordained with such severe earnestness.

20. God requires earnestness from man in return for receiving this Testament: not with cold, lukewarm hearts, merely to cover over sin with the sufferings of Christ and comfort oneself with it while remaining in **Impenitency** <note>The state of not feeling regret or remorse for errors or sins</note>.

21. It is not a forgiveness from the outside, which is credited to man **from the outside**. No, it is through Christ's blood and death; when the poor soul penetrates into that, then the sufferings, dying, and **Resurrection**, together with the satisfaction in this Testamentary Covenant, are put onto the soul in the blood of Christ. This kills Sin, Death, and Hell, and leads the poor soul to the Father, in Christ.

22. It is not enough for a man to know that Christ died for sin, and to **assent to it**, hold it for true, and receive the satisfaction as a **work done**. No, no, there is no such receiving; rather, the whole man must give himself into it. He must will to die in Christ's death to his own evil natural will, as well as to wicked lusts. Then Christ puts his victory and satisfaction upon him, and the true heavenly Spirit springs forth through Christ's death in his Resurrection—like a beautiful flower out of the wild earth. There, a true Christian is born, who is a branch on the vine of Christ, as seen in **John 15**.

23. Now, there belongs no great art or skill to this, but only a child-

...a childlike simplicity and *humility*: the plowman is as near to it as the doctor <note>original: "Doctor," referring here to a learned scholar or teacher of theology</note>; they must all enter into humility in the simplicity of Christ, and come like the lost son <note>The Prodigal Son (Luke 15)</note> and the tax collector in the temple <note>The Publican (Luke 18)</note>; there is no other way to it.

24. There is no need for great speculation about it, or concerning what polished words or behavior a man uses to approach it. We must only come as children, who yield to the father under his rod <note>A reference to accepting divine discipline</note> and pray for grace.

25. He who has learned much, and knows how to prepare himself for it, is no more acceptable to God than he who knows nothing, but yet with his whole *heart* and soul turns to Him in sorrow for sins, having faith in grace and a true, earnest purpose and resolution to be a new creature.

26. This foundation is only brought forth so deeply so that everyone who strives for it might see the true inward ground, cease from strife, and yield himself into the simplicity and love of *Jesus Christ*. By doing so, the power of *Satan* will suddenly be diminished, and people and nations will see that Christians are the children of God if they walk in love in this way. I wish for this from my heart, and for this cause, this little book is written.

# The Preface.

### Note.
### Here the Reader who loves God should know:

First, that these two little books were copied faithfully and diligently from the author's own blessed manuscript, just as he wrote them in the year 1623.

And secondly, that afterward in the year 1624—at the request of some good friends and lovers of the Truth—he intended to rewrite them both in a more "child-like" <note>original: "child-like forme"; meaning a simpler, more accessible style</note> format for the better understanding of the unlearned. This is evident in the previously mentioned letter to Carol von Endern. However, he only completed this revision up to part of the third chapter of this first book, and no further. This is what the publisher of the High German <note>original: "High dutch"</note> copy writes.

But more particularly, let the reader take notice that the author had proceeded to the 17th verse of the 3rd chapter, as can be seen in the printing of the double pages in this book. It was printed this way in the High German edition, and in the same manner here in the English edition. The only difference is the size of the font. Those words which are the same in both versions are printed in a font of the same size as the rest of the book. However, on the first side, any text that does not appear in the other version is printed in a smaller font; this represents the first book that was written and finished by the author. On the second side, any text not found in the first version is printed in a larger font; this represents the second copy which he intended to finish throughout the entire book. This has been done to distinguish, to some extent, the changes in expression between the two different copies for a better understanding of the Mystery <note>original: "Mystery"; likely referring to the divine or mystical revelations contained in the work</note>. If this is diligently observed, it will greatly help in understanding this book and all of his other writings.

<summary>This preface explains the editorial history of the text, noting that it combines the author's original 1623 manuscript with a partially completed 1624 revision intended to simplify the material.</summary>
<keywords>Carol von Endern, High German, manuscript, publishing history, typography, Jacob Boehme, biblical commentary</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the preface's discussion on the interpretation of the author's writings and the necessity of spiritual experience over mere academic learning.</meta>

# The Preface.

writings, *because where the expression is obscure in one, it is clearer in the other. Besides, by varying the expression, everyone may discern what latitude the author's words may allow for in their meaning throughout all his books.*

Further, *it is certain that we may be led by these various expressions to the true knowledge of some part of the Mystery <note>The "Mystery" refers to deep spiritual truths or divine revelations often discussed in mystical theology.</note>; and this great benefit results from it: it will enable us to speak and write with certainty about things that have never been spoken or written concerning that Mystery, whether in divine or natural matters.*

But by the way, *this is not to be attained by outward hearing or reading alone, but by experience. Teachers or hearers who lack experience are indeed "ordinary" <note>original: "Ordinary"; here meaning common or conventional in a derogatory sense.</note> teachers and hearers, but not true ones—for true ones are considered "extraordinary" in the opinion of our times. Such true hearers are all those who understand the Word through feeling and bring forth fruit in their lives and conduct, possessing the power of godliness. These can teach truly from what they know, even if only in a small measure. But those who are called ordinary teachers, who have only a form of godliness and deny its power, cannot teach at all; they do not even deserve the name of an ordinary teacher. Such a person is not even a true hearer.*

*Our Savior teaches us that whoever will do the will of our Father who is* <margin>John 7:17</margin> *in Heaven, they shall know of his words, whether they be of God. And if He submits His doctrine to be examined by everyone by this rule, why should not every word of all others be examined by the same rule? Let us do this, and we shall commonly attain that which is indeed extraordinary: the true knowledge of spiritual things—the things of God—which are impossible to be perceived by the natural man <note>original: "Naturall man"; a biblical term for a person relying on human reason without spiritual inspiration.</note>. Accordingly, I desire you to judge the author of this book by this standard, and no other. Farewell.*

<summary>The author argues that spiritual truth is found through personal experience and "the power of godliness" rather than formal education, urging readers to judge the text by its spiritual fruits.</summary>

<header>The First Chapter</header>

# Concerning Reason’s view of itself, and how it tends to function in a creaturely way when it considers Christ and his Testaments.

### I.

All strife and *misunderstanding* concerning Christ’s *Person* and the *Testaments* <note>original: "Testaments"; referring here to the Sacraments, specifically Baptism and the Lord's Supper</note> which he left behind him, arises from defective, creaturely reason. This reason desires to be the mistress of all things or beings, yet it looks only at the multiplicity and the variety of beings. In such a view, reason only loses itself and breaks away from its center or origin. It disperses the <margin>Or, senses.</margin> thoughts into a multitude of things, so that they cannot see the ground from which they have sprung. Thus, in their confusion and wandering, they break themselves off from their *Chaos* <note>original: "Chaos"; used here in the classical sense of the "infinite void" or the unformed primordial state</note>—namely, from the Eternal WORD of God and from the eternal divine speaking. In this spoken Word, all beings—along with *understanding, reason,* and *thoughts*—exist, and from it they take their foundation and beginning.

2. For if the abyssal <note>original: "Abyssall"; meaning "from the abyss," referring to God as the "Unground" or the infinite depth beyond human measurement</note>, non-natural, and uncreated GOD—namely, the Eternal ONE—should speak his WORD no more, and *that speaking should cease*, there would be no more understanding, reason, or thoughts. There would also be no nature or creature, and all beings would be an eternal nothing. For every life arises from the exhalation <note>original: "Exhalation"; the "breathing out" or manifestation of God's spirit</note> of the Eternal ONE, namely, from the *Abyss*. Without this, there could be no capacity for form within the Eternal One, according to which, or out of which, something might be made.

3. For if there were a predetermined form or figure, then there must also have been a cause from which that form arose, and God would not be the one and only God, who is without ground, time, and place. Everything that has a beginning has a *ground* <note>original: "Ground"; a basis or cause for its existence</note>; but that which has no beginning is without ground and form.

4. Every beginning proceeds out of the Eternal ONE, through the exhalation of the Eternal ONE. By this, the Eternal ONE brings itself into a self-viewing perceptibility and "findability," leading to the moving and forming of itself. Every visible and invisible being, spiritual and corporeal, has taken its origin in the exhalation of the Eternal ONE, and stands with its foundation therein; for—

<summary>The author argues that religious conflict stems from human reason losing sight of its divine origin in the Eternal Word. He explains that all existence is a "breathing out" or manifestation of the one, formless, and infinite God.</summary>
<keywords>Reason, Christ's Testaments, Eternal Word, The Abyss, The One, Creation, Divine Speaking, Metaphysics</keywords>

--- Page 36 ---
<meta>This is the opening chapter of the treatise, establishing the philosophical and theological groundwork for understanding Christ's nature and the sacraments.</meta>

# How Reason is accustomed to view itself in a creaturely <note>original: "Creaturely," meaning relating to the limited perspective of a created being</note> image-likeness when it considers Christ and his Testaments.

<margin>In an imaginary manner.</margin>

### From where the strife about Christ’s Testaments arises; and how this strife is an empty, unprofitable thing.

All strife and *misunderstanding* concerning Christ’s *Person, Office,* and *Being* or substance—as well as concerning his *Testaments* <note>The "Testaments" here refer to the Sacraments, specifically Baptism and the Lord's Supper</note> which he left behind him, wherein he works <sup>b</sup> *presently*—arises from defective, creaturely *Reason*. This reason operates only in an **image-like opinion** and does not reach the **Ground** <note>original: "Ground" (German: *Grund*), referring to the underlying essence or foundation of reality</note> of this mystery. Yet, this reason insists on being a mistress of all things or beings and seeks to **judge** all things. In doing so, it only loses itself in such image-likeness, breaks itself off from its center, and disperses its <sup>c</sup> thoughts. It runs on into multiplicity, by which its Ground is confused and the mind is disquieted, and thus it does not know itself.

<margin>At present.</margin>
<margin>Inward senses or thoughts.</margin>

No life can remain in certainty unless it continues in its center, from which it originated.

Since the soul originated from God’s Word and Will, yet has entered into its own lust and desire to will for itself, it cannot reach its *first Ground* from which it sprang while searching through such self-willing. Consequently, it runs outside of its Ground in mere uncertainty until it returns to its origin again.

Every beginning goes out of the Eternal ONE—namely, out of the *Tri-unity* of GOD—through the exhalation <note>original: "Exhalation," the "breathing out" or speaking of the Word (Logos)</note> or speaking of the unity of God, just as a fountain flows from its source. Through this flowing forth, the unity brings itself into self-viewing, discovery, and perceptibility, for the purpose of forming and imaging itself. Every visible and invisible being, both spiritual and corporeal, has taken its origin in the exhalation of the Divine Power, and is a <sup>d</sup> reflection.

<margin>Or, resemblance or antitype.</margin>

<summary>The author argues that religious conflict arises because human reason relies on superficial mental images rather than the divine "Ground" of reality. He asserts that the soul must return to its origin in the Divine Unity to find certain truth.</summary>

# Of the Original of the Strife. Chapter 1.

The beginning of every being is nothing else but an *imagination* of the *Abyss* <note>original: *Abysse*; referring to the "Unground" or the infinite, bottomless void of the divine origin</note>, in that the same brings itself by its own longing into an **imagination**, and **models** and **images** itself, and apprehends that image-likeness, and <margin>Or, *exhales it.*</margin> breathes it forth from the Eternal One to a viewing of itself.

5. Which breathing is the Eternal Word of the Abyssal Deity; a speaking forth of the *Abyss* into a ground, of the unsubstantial into a substantial. In this, the whole Creation—along with the speaking forth, as in the divisibility <note>original: *seperability*; the capacity of the one Word to divide into many distinct things</note> of the speaking—has taken its beginning, and does yet evermore take it. And every life consists in that divisibility of the speaking, where the modeled imagination, in the exhalation, parts itself into distinctness. In this parting, the sensibility of the only life is understood, where the One views itself in the multiplicity.

6. Also, herein is to be understood the ground of the *properties* <note>The specific qualities or characteristics of nature</note>, in that the parting of the only longing brings itself into desire, and <margin>Comprehends.</margin> encloses and makes itself essential or substantial. In this <margin>Comprehensibility.</margin> inclusiveness, the *seven forms of Nature* take their beginning, as is sufficiently declared in our other writings.

7. Therefore I say, the cause that men dispute and strive about **God**, about his *Word, Essence, or Being, and Will*, is that the understanding has broken itself off from its *center* or *ground*. This breaking off is nothing else but that the properties (which have gone forth out of the eternal out-speaking of the *Word* into a creaturely life) have brought themselves into self-lust <note>Self-centered desire or ego</note>, to the out-speaking of themselves. In their own self-conceived lust, they have broken themselves off from the <margin>Or, *after.*</margin> eternal longing toward the Word of the eternal speaking, and brought themselves into a private sensibility of nature, and confused themselves in the forms of nature. There, all the thoughts or senses will dwell and run without the only God in self-speculation and *reason*; they can in no way come to their *center* or ground, unless they sink down into themselves in their speculation, and go again into the ground out of which they existed, and fall again into the eternal speaking Word, and give their own wills into the eternal speaking Word, so that the same own-will of the *creaturely life* may be spoken out with, and in, the eternal speaking Word in the divisibility of the Word.

8. In which re-speaking, the new *regeneration* of the *human life* and will is understood. For the human life was, in the beginning of man, in the *Word of God*, and by the in-breathing of the Word into the *human body* was manifested, and came into sensibility, perceptibility, and willing. Then the willing broke itself off from the Word—in which the life was, *without creature* <note>Existing in God before being a physical being</note>—and brought itself into a self-separation and visibility of its perceptibility of the five senses. In this sensibility it now at present runs, and seeks the seat of God there, but finds only a measurability, and natural and creaturely form; wherein

For the beginning of every being is nothing else but an imagination of the *out-flowing* **will** of God, which has brought itself into separability, form, and image-likeness; wherein lies the whole Creation. Seeing then that the human life is an out-flowing and reflection of the divine power, understanding, and skill, therefore the same ought to continue in its origin; otherwise, it *loses* the *divine* knowledge, power, and skill. By focusing only on itself, it brings itself into **centers of its own and strange imaging**, by which its origin becomes darkened and foreign.

This is evidenced by *erroneous reason*, which always teaches concerning **GOD** and yet has no true understanding; also, it never comes to rest while it runs on in strange imaging.

Therefore, I say that this is the only cause why men dispute and strive about God, His Word, essence or being, and **will**: that the *understanding* of man has broken itself off from its origin and now runs on in mere self-will, thoughts, and images in its own desire for selfishness. It imagines for itself another foundation for the divine willing, in which there is yet no *true* knowledge—nor can there be—until the life returns into its origin: namely, into the divine out-flowing and **will.**

# Of the Origin of the Strife. Chapter 1.

Now it strives within its own *center*. For its own will has brought itself into its own *center* and broken itself off from the whole; as to the total, it has become as if it were dead.

9. Therefore Christ says, *Unless you are converted and become as children, and are newborn through water and the Spirit, you cannot see the Kingdom of God* (Matthew 18:4; John 3:5, 7). The individual will should return again into its "nothingness" <note>The state of having no self-centered desires</note>, and then it stands again in the first *birth* and will again be spoken forth from the Eternal Word into a Divine Will. For whatever exists—whose living and willing acts or runs without the Eternal speaking Word—that same thing is outside of eternity and lives merely in *time*.

10. But seeing that the soul has its origin out of the Eternal Word as a power of it, it cannot rest in the being or essence of time. Instead, it seeks its own *Mother* <note>The divine source or wisdom</note> who generated it and brought it into a creaturely form; but its own wandering makes it so that it cannot find its Mother.

11. Therefore, all strife concerning *Divine Mysteries* is a useless thing. It is done from the outside, without God, in self-perception, where the senses view themselves in nature in a creaturely form. There is no comprehension or true understanding or knowledge of God unless "image-like" reason <note>Reason that only deals with mental images or concepts rather than reality</note> abandons itself and sinks down with its own will into its center again, out of which it first came: namely, into the Eternal Speaking Word of God. It must receive that speaking or breathing of God into itself again and, through divine science <note>original: *science*; here meaning spiritual knowledge or skill</note>, speak in a <margin>f Distinguishable.</margin> f separable and sensible form. It must be a dwelling and temple of God, wherein God’s will works, governs, and wills. Otherwise, there is no true knowledge or skill concerning God and His being or essence.

12. For no spirit knows God or His word and will unless God’s word and will are manifested and stirring within it. Natural reason, without the light of God, sees only natural mental images; it goes on in its own speculation and frames for itself the *Divine Being or Essence* as if it were just such a thing. From this has come the strife among those learned in reason, so that men strive and dispute about God and about His being, essence, and will. Each of them holds his own imagination to be *divine* and wants his own *image*—which he has framed in the imagination of his reason—to be honored as God. Yet it is only a natural image of reason; and thus men strive all the world over about these *images of reason*.

13. But a true man, who stands rightly in the image of God, has no strife in religion. For he lives in his first Mother, who has formed him—soul, spirit, and body—and his whole substance into an image. He wills and acts with her; he is resigned <note>Submitted or surrendered</note> to her and yields his will to her, and she feeds and nourishes him. Every property of the true, resigned man is nourished with its like. First, the body, made from the *slime* <note>original: *limus*; the dust or clay of the earth</note> of the earth, is nourished...

# Chapter 1. Concerning Christ’s Testaments.

The life flows forth through the person in the same kind and manner as it did in the *Beginning*, when it flowed from divine power and skill.

As Christ has taught us when he says, *Unless you are converted and become as a little child, you shall not enter the kingdom of God* <note>Matthew 18:3</note>. That is, the life must turn itself back toward **God**, from whom it proceeded, and must forsake all its own **imagining** and **lust** <note>original: "Lust"; here meaning selfish desire or worldly craving</note>. In doing so, it returns to the **Divine Vision** once again.

All strife regarding the **Divine Will** and **Being** or **Essence**—through which men despise one another—comes from "self-imagelikeness." This occurs when one man tries to grasp the "image" of another (that is, **his thoughts**) and yet cannot rightly understand them. This happens where one man inserts himself into the thoughts and mind of another, bringing his own biases into them. He compels another person's thoughts into his own meaning and forcibly elevates himself over the thoughts and mind of another. He claims those thoughts as his own property and wishes to make a grand show of them, using them to dominate the thoughts and minds of others. He demands that men must worship and reverence his own thoughts and mind, holding them to be the **Word** of God, or <margin>Or, Oracles.</margin> the speaking of God.

In this way, man deludes himself and **robs God** of His honor. He *takes God's covenant into his mouth* and *yet hates the discipline of the Spirit of God*. Therefore, the Spirit reproves him in his conscience, showing him that he is nothing but an **apostate Lucifer** <note>A reference to the fallen pride of the devil</note> who seeks to force others into his own **image**, demanding they accept it as the **Word of God**.

But a true man **inclines himself toward his Original** <note>God, the source of his being</note> and forsakes all **images**. He desires no "self-imageability" <note>The tendency to create one's own mental idols or self-centered interpretations</note> of his understanding, except for what **God** will frame and speak with and through him. He despiseth no one,

16. Into whatever the spirit of the will brings itself with its imagination, so that it impresses and comprehends it, therein also it *images* itself into being or essence. For no spirit can bring anything to pass without being or essence. If the Eternal One were not *essential* <note>having actual substance or being</note>, all would be nothing; and if that ONE had not a will, there would be no desire, nor power, nor word, nor essence.

17. Indeed, we acknowledge that the will of the <term>Abyss</term> <note>the "Ungrund" or bottomless void before creation</note> has brought itself into a longing and imagination of itself, from which *Nature* and *Creature* have their origin. From this also the natural life has its origin, which now also—out of the <term>partibleness</term> <note>the quality of being divided into parts</note> of the exhaled will—has its own *will* and imagination to form and image itself according to its longing and desire. We see such changing in Nature: how Nature images itself into so many kinds and properties, and how those imaged properties every one desire their like again.

18. Seeing then we understand in man, that he especially above all other creatures desires and longs after three properties: First, he longs according to his <gloss>b</gloss> <margin>b Sensible or conceptive.</margin> apprehensive understanding after the hidden God; and though indeed he sees Him not with bodily eyes, yet he desires Him. Secondly, he longs after his *Astrum* or constellation, out of which the mind and rational life proceeded; therefore the rational life longs again after its mother. Thirdly, he longs after the *stars* or powers of the earth and the other *elements*, and desires them for his nourishment. And therefore we know also by this—

# Chapter 1. About Christ’s Testament.

But it only distinguishes the true from the false, the good from the evil, and teaches the truth with a powerful, divine outflowing and will.

All disputation concerning God’s being, essence, and will is performed in the images of the senses or thoughts without God. For if anyone lives in God and wills with God, why does he need to dispute about God—who or what God is?

But the fact that he disputes about it is a sign that he has never felt it at all in his mind or senses; it has not been granted to him that God is in him and wills what he wills. It is a certain sign that he wishes to exalt his own opinion and image above others, and that he desires dominion.

Men should friendly confer together and offer one another their gifts and knowledge in *love*, and *test things with one another*, and *hold fast to that which is best*, <margin>1 Thessalonians 5:21.</margin> They should friendly *instruct* one another and not stand so firmly in their own opinion as if they could not err. We must realize we have a mighty enemy against us, who suddenly brings strange images into man’s thoughts and makes men *insulting* <note>original: "Insult"; here meaning to behave with insolent triumph or arrogance</note>; this is how sects and schisms <note>original: "Scismses"; formal divisions or separations within a church</note> come to exist.

It lies in no single man’s person such that people should suppose that divine understanding must come only from such and such a person. For the Scripture says: *Test all things, and hold fast to that which is good* (1 Thessalonians 5:21).

The touchstone <note>a standard or criterion by which something is judged or recognized</note> for this knowledge is, first, the cornerstone, Christ: men should see whether a thing enters out of love into love, or whether the love of God alone is purely sought and desired; whether it is done out of humility or pride. Secondly, whether it is according to the *Holy Scripture* of the Bible. Thirdly, is it according to the human heart and soul, wherein the book of the life of God is incorporated?

<summary>The author argues that true spiritual knowledge is found in love and humility rather than intellectual debate, warning that religious pride leads to sectarianism. He provides a "touchstone" for discerning truth based on Christ's love, Scripture, and the internal witness of the soul.</summary>

# Of the Original of the Strife. Chapter 1.

This hunger of his <note>referring to man's spiritual desire</note> is such that he must have his origin out of these *three* <note>the three principles of the divine essence often discussed by Boehme</note>. For there is also such a threefold *spirit* to be understood in him, arising from such an origin. And then there is also such a threefold *essence* or substance in which his spirit works, where every operation longs after its first mother and receives its nourishment from her.

19. But seeing the soul—namely, the inward ground of man—in *Adam* the first man, has turned away from its first mother (its first origin), which is the divine *Word* and Will. With its longing and desire, it has turned out into the operation of the stars <note>original: "constellation"</note> and elements. It formed itself in that operation and plowed itself into a *strange imagination* <note>term: "imagination" here refers to the soul's creative focus or what it "images" itself into</note>, whereby the divine food—namely, the *essential wisdom of God*—is withdrawn. Because the soul has broken itself off from this wisdom with its longing desire, it is now wholly blind toward *God*, and the first *divine essence* in which God created it has departed.

20. When the soul brought its imagination out of that [divine essence or substance] and into the *earthly* and astral property, then its body also, in which the threefold spirit works, became wholly earthly, gross, and beast-like. For into whatever the imagination of the spirit brings itself, such a body also becomes, through the impression of the spiritual desire. As we see in man, he has gained a gross earthly body in which the principles now stand in mere strife, contrariety, and *enmity*. From this, pain, <margin>Fragility.</margin> corruptibility, and death exist. Yet God forbade him this in Paradise while he still stood there, *that he should not eat of the knowledge of evil and good* with the imagination; otherwise, he would fall into such necessity, misery, and death, and die to the Kingdom of Heaven—as has indeed come to pass (*Genesis* 2:17).

21. When the soul brought itself into the earthly imagination, it brought itself into the earthly *image* and lost the *heavenly image*. Whereas it should have imaged itself into the essential *wisdom*—namely, into the holy *heavenly essential Word*—and taken its nourishment from there, it instead imaged itself in the outward stars <note>original: "astrum"</note> and in the serpent's and devil's desire. By this, in its noble image, it became a mask <note>original: "vizard"</note> and *monster* in the sight of God. It lost the angelic form which it had, as well as Paradise and the Kingdom of Heaven. Now, with its foundation standing in the impression of *darkness* in the anger of God, it must have remained forever in such a masked image if the great love of God had not come to help it again. If the divine Word—namely, its first mother and origin—had not again *inspired* or spoken *grace* into it, the soul would be lost. This same Word, with its most inward secrecy and love, would give itself again into the ground of the soul as a new wellspring and fountain, bringing the soul *new nourishment* for its *life*. By this, its natural fiery and painful property becomes changed into the image of God again.

# Chapter 1. About Christ's Testaments.

And these writings may very well be read by the children of God; for where the true mind has its touchstone <note>original: "Touch-stone"; a metaphor for a standard or criterion used to test the purity of gold, here meaning a spiritual intuition</note> within itself, it can distinguish all things. If it is so that the Holy Spirit dwells in the foundation of the mind, that person has a sufficient touchstone, *which will lead him into all truth.*

Christ’s Testaments <note>referring here to the Sacraments, specifically Baptism and the Lord's Supper</note> are a secret mystery. They are offered to the minds and thoughts that have turned away from sin and returned to God. Where the life brings itself back to God, the returning thoughts will first be fed with divine power and understanding. This afterwards kindles the life so that it hungers after God. To such a person, Christ’s flesh and blood are given as a <margin>Or, a pawn or earnest money.</margin> **pledge** and **seal**, and the divine essence or substance will be imprinted within them. By this, the life is brought back to its origin: namely, into God’s *power* and *word*.

A false thought or mind in a person is nourished by its own likeness—that is, from its own guesswork, from pride of mind, or from the subtlety of the serpent. These are the things it desires to exalt and form into an image; and that image is a branch on the tree of Satan.

Now, the person who is fed by divine power and spirit in his mind and thoughts is divinely minded, and brings forth good things out of his good heart.

But the person who is fed in his thoughts by the power and will of the flesh is *only fleshly minded*.

When *reason* <note>The author distinguishes between "Reason" (human logic) and "Understanding" (divine revelation).</note> considers Christ’s Testaments and ponders how Christ can be present in his Testaments, it thinks it is done in an <margin>Imaginary.</margin> **image-like manner** <note>meaning a symbolic or figurative representation rather than a literal reality</note>.

And when reason realizes that it is not done in a symbolic or image-like manner, it then falls away from the truth entirely,
