# Chapter 1. Concerning Christ’s Testaments.

And they think he is present only in *remembrance*: as when his Word is preached, then he works only in the *same* powerfully. And so they also think concerning his testaments <note>original: "Testaments"; referring here to the sacraments of Baptism and the Lord's Supper</note>, that Christ works only spiritually *in the faith*, and that the testaments are *only* signs or symbols, by which we should instruct ourselves in what he has done for us. They believe we should only use them to declare his death and the shedding of his blood, and should keep it in lively remembrance for our comfort.

Thus, human reason understands nothing at all of the *kingdom* of Christ, much less of his *person* or of his *office*. In that respect, men dispute and strive about it, and insist on attaining it through the searching of reason. All of this fails to reach the true *understanding*. For Christ’s testaments are heavenly, and reason is earthly and worldly. Reason seeks Christ in time (or in that which is temporary), and if it does not find him there according to its own power, then it supposes he is present 

<margin>Or, senses.</margin>

only to the *thoughts*, which cast themselves up to him in heaven. But this will *not* renew the life and bring it into God again; it will *not* create the new birth.

All strife comes from this: that men do not understand that heaven wherein Christ *sits at the right hand of God*. They do not understand that he is in this world, and that the world stands in heaven, and heaven in the *world*, and that they are in one another, as day and night.

The inward ground of the Word, out of which the *four elements* <note>The traditional elements of Earth, Water, Air, and Fire, which the author views as emerging from a spiritual source.</note> have sprung, is the heaven—namely, a spiritual world. In that inward power, Christ rules as true God and—

<summary>The author argues that human reason incorrectly views the sacraments as mere symbols of remembrance, failing to grasp that Christ is substantially present through a spiritual reality that interpenetrates the physical world.</summary>
<keywords>sacraments, reason vs. faith, omnipresence of Christ, new birth, spiritual world, four elements, theology of presence</keywords>

# Chapter 1. Concerning Christ’s Testaments.

and Man, through the Outward World: For where Christ says in Matthew 28:18, 20: *All Power is given to Me in Heaven and on Earth: Also; I am with you always, even until the End of the World:* Also; *He shall rule over all his Enemies, until all his Enemies are laid under him as a footstool*, 1 Corinthians 15:25; Psalm 110:1. This is to be understood of his inward Kingdom, where, in the inward Power, he rules over the outward Earthly realm, and also the Hellish realm.

For the outward World has sprung out of the inward Spiritual World; namely: out of Light and Darkness. This structure, before the Office of Christ, existed in the Eternal Creator’s Office; he who from Eternity has worked <note>original: "wrought"</note> light and darkness, namely: the Spiritual World. This structure has flowed forth and become visible, and was brought by God into a Creation, wherein Light and Darkness—that is, Good and Evil—rule alongside one another. In this, *Hell* and Pain are understood as the flowing forth of the Eternal Darkness; and the *Light* of Nature is understood as the flowing forth of the Light. In that realm where Evil and Good rule alike, we understand the Kingdom of *Nature*, with hot and cold, and all other properties.

God has given this Dominion to the *Office* <note>The "Office of Christ" refers to his functional role as Mediator and King of the universe.</note> of Christ, so that he, being both true God and Man, should rule over all the characteristics <note>original: "Propriety"</note> and self-will of this Kingdom, where Evil and Good rule within one another. 

Just as the Sun in the visible World rules over Evil and Good, and is present everywhere with its light and power (and all that it is), penetrating into every Being—and yet, in its distinct form <note>original: "Image-like forme"</note>, it does not pull things away to itself as it shines out <note>original: "Efflux"</note>, but wholly gives itself into every Being, and yet always remains whole, with nothing of its Being departing from it—so also it is to be understood concerning Christ’s Person and Office. He rules in the Inward Spiritual World visibly, and in the Outward World Invisibly, and through—

# Of the Original Cause of the Strife. Chapter 1.

Through death (and having made death into life), Christ has become a Lord over sin, death, the devil, and hell. He has carried all of them in his Resurrection as a <margin>show or triumph</margin> spectacle over the human soul and body, acting as a victor over them.

28. Thus, by my power, they may go through death and be likewise transformed, coming again to you through my Resurrection. Just as I have arisen from the dead and have brought the humanity I assumed from them back to you—so that I, as true God and man in one person, am one with you and have possessed the throne of glory—so, Father, I also will that those whom you have given me be where I am and see my glory."*

29. Now, we see that Christ has said in *John 6* that he is the bread that has come down from heaven to give life to the world, and that we should eat his flesh and drink his blood. Also, in *John 4*, he said he would give us the water of eternal life, and whoever should drink of it would thirst no more; instead, it would flow in him as a fountain of eternal life, and streams of living water should flow from him.

30. # Chapter 1. Concerning Christ's Testament.

The power of Christ deeply penetrates the faithful man's soul, spirit, and heart. Just as fire glows through iron, and as the sun works through and through a herb so that the herb becomes "solar"—or filled with the virtue of the sun, and as it were so converted by the sun that it becomes wholly of the nature of the sun—so Christ rules in the resigned will <note>original: *gelassenheit*; a state of yielding one's will to God</note>, in soul and body, over all evil inclinations and over the lust introduced by Satan. He generates the man to be a new heavenly creature and wholly flows into him with his own self, both in divine and human power. Thus, the faithful man becomes a true branch on his vine, in which God and man (as to that same inward new birth) dwell together.

Now, Christ's Testaments consist of this: that he offers himself to <gloss>faith</gloss> <margin>The German text uses *Glauben*. See Chapter 2, verse 1 on the second side.</margin>, and that he will give it his flesh and blood and all grace. He will spiritually dwell in man, just as the sun dwells in a plant and makes the fruit ripe and <gloss>tender</gloss> <margin>Or, supple.</margin>.

In like manner, the poor, fallen, earthly man—who perished in soul and body—is renewed again and worked upon to become a heavenly fruit. In the end, only the coarseness of the flesh falls away from him, while the spirit together with the soul remains in Christ. Even here in this life, according to this indwelling power, he *dwells in heaven*; concerning which St. Paul says: *Our citizenship <note>original: "conversation," from the Latin "conversatio," meaning one's way of life or citizenship</note> is in heaven* (Philippians 3:20). But the body is in the world and of the world's essence. And just as the heavens penetrate the world and give it virtue and power, so also Christ thoroughly penetrates the outward man with his inward ruling power and resists the vain lusts of the earthly nature.

Therefore, I say: no one understands anything of God unless God works it in his mind and <gloss>thoughts</gloss> <margin>Or, senses.</margin>. For all natural knowledge is outward in the world; it arises from its *astrum* <note>term: *astrum* refers to the "star" or inner constellation of a person's nature and the celestial influences upon them</note> and continues on in mere guessing and doubting.

<summary>This page explains the mystical union between Christ and the believer, using the metaphor of the sun ripening a plant to describe how divine grace penetrates and transforms human nature. It emphasizes that true spiritual understanding comes only through God's internal work rather than natural human reason.</summary>

### The Second Chapter.

## Concerning the Institution of *Baptism*: what it is, who it is that baptizes, and what is baptized; and how <margin>Or, Baptism of Water.</margin> *Water-Baptism* is to be understood.

-> I. <-

When anyone wants to kindle a fire, they must have some substance that will catch fire. It must be a substance containing oil and *water*, or it will not burn. For if they cast a stone or something similar into the fire, it will not burn in a way that produces a <margin>Blazing.</margin> shining light. 

The same is to be understood concerning the soul. The soul broke its desire away from God’s essence of love and meekness. This divine meekness was within the soul like a spiritual oil and <margin>Assuaged or refreshed.</margin> fountain of water, which *allayed* <note>original: "allayed"; meaning to cool or temper the heat</note> its fiery source; through this, its fire possessed a shining and light. However, when the soul turned away, its own "Separator"—namely the natural *Fiat* <note>The "Fiat" refers to the creative word or "let it be" of God that forms and solidifies matter</note>—<margin>Impressed or compacted.</margin> compressed itself. By such compression, its properties became like a hard stone.

<summary>This chapter begins an exploration of Baptism by using the metaphor of fire, oil, and water to explain how the soul lost its divine light and became hardened when it separated from God's love.</summary>

->The Second Chapter.<-

### Concerning God’s Covenant after the Fall: what the Fall of Man is, and how God has bound and united himself with him again. What *Circumcision* in the Old Testament and *Baptism* in the New are.

<note>The woodcut initial 'W' begins the following paragraph.</note>

When anyone wants to kindle a fire, they must have some substance that will catch fire. It must be a substance containing oil and sulfur <note>original: "Brimstone"</note>, or it will not burn. For if they cast a stone or something similar into the fire, it will not burn in a way that produces a shining light. It is to be understood the same way concerning the poor soul: when it broke its desire away from God’s essence of love and meekness—which essence was in it in the beginning as a spiritual oil and water—it became like a spiritual sulfur. In that original state, the light of God burned within it; but then the soul brought itself into its own selfish desire. By doing this, it was shut up like a *hard stone* and lost all its love and meekness. It became like a burning sulfurous spirit, which could not be remedied unless the oil of divine meekness and love flowed into it again.

->*What does this anointing signify now?*<-

Answer: Nothing else but that as man is *tinctured* <note>original: "tinctured"; meaning spiritually infused or transformed</note> again in soul and body, and so thoroughly penetrated and healed that he is capable of receiving divine power again—namely, the divine fire of love. Therefore, his "brimstone spirit" <note>A term used by Boehme to describe the harsh, acidic, or "sulfurous" quality of the fallen human soul</note> of the wrathful, fiery soul, which had perished in relation to God, must be remedied again by the covenant of God through such an *anointing oil*. As with baptism, the *water* of the eternal life of divine meekness is poured into it; and in the Supper, the *fire-burning* love in the life of our Lord Jesus Christ is poured into it.

->*Reason says:*<-
->*Cannot God forgive man his sin without a medium?*<-

Answer: The matter was not about forgiveness alone. The soul needed not only forgiveness, but a *New Birth*. It had brought itself into its "own-will" <note>Self-will or ego, acting independently of God</note> and quite broken itself off from God’s will, from which eternal darkness emerged within it. For the source of its life—namely, its own "separator" <note>original: "Seperator"; the internal principle that divides and distinguishes qualities within a being</note> (understand this as the cause of its creaturely movement and life)—had lifted itself up, and the properties...

He breathed into that source of meekness which the soul once had, but which was no longer active.

7. Into this soul, the movable power spoke itself again to be a new *center* and covenant: that in the fullness of time, God would introduce and manifest his living *Ens*—the essential word of his power—into this spoken voice of grace and into man’s vanished heavenly essence.

In this way, the fire and light become a clearer radiance, no longer shut up (as it lies shut up within the essence of wood).

They did this so that <margin>Or, forming an image.</margin> their imagination might form, mold, or image itself in the figure of Christ, so that the spirit of their will might stand in a modeling <note>original: "Modelling"; the process of being shaped or configured to a spiritual pattern</note> wherein it might work within grace.

# Chapter 2. And the Baptism in the New Testament.

Namely: the Living Word of his Power and Wisdom, with the most high Love, specifically the Name JESUS, and therewith make the vanished Essence or Substance living and growing again. From this, the Soul should again eat of the heavenly Substance, whereby its anxious fire-source <note>The author uses "fire-source" to describe the soul's raw, restless energy before it is tempered by divine love.</note> would again be changed into a Love-fire. *The dry Rod of Aaron, mentioned by Moses, which grew again and bore almonds, was a figure <note>original: "Bild" or "Type"; a symbolic representation of a future reality.</note> of this.*

<margin>The Text. Image or Type.</margin>
This *inspoken* Grace-word of the Serpent-Destroyer <note>A reference to the promised Savior who would defeat the serpent from Genesis.</note> is what the souls of the holy children of God *before the Incarnation* of Christ kindled. They believed God and his *promise* of the fulfillment that was to come, and in *this faith* they performed sacrifices.

For their sacrifices, especially those of the first Fathers after *Adam*, were intended only to represent an image or figure of how the soul should be sacrificed in the fire of God’s wrath. They showed how the soul, through this inspoken ground of Grace and Covenant, should be changed within the fire of God’s wrath into a Love-fire. It showed how the soul should enter into death and a dying to its own self-will of false desire; how the false will should be burned away from it; and how—in the power of this inspoken grace of the love and meekness of God—it should spring up or go forth through the *fire* into a clear *light*. Thus, it becomes a newborn child, which is no longer dark but light. It also lived no more in its own will, but in God’s will. 

The sacrifices also showed how the introduced serpent’s poison in this transmutation <note>original: "transmutation"; a complete change of nature or substance.</note> separates itself from the soul, in the same way that smoke separates itself from fire and light. In that process, the fire and light become a clearer radiance and are no longer shut up, as they are shut up within the essence of *wood*. Even so, the (Holy) Divine fire of the soul was also shut up through sin, which *no one* could unlock and kindle except for the Love of God in this incorporated Grace-Covenant.

This image, type, or figure they set before themselves with the sacrifices, looking toward the future fulfillment. They introduced their faith along with the inspoken grace of the "Woman’s seed" and Serpent-Destroyer, so that their *faith* might thus form or mold itself in the figure of Christ. In this way, the spirit of their will might stand in the figure and image of Christ, and their faith might work within that incorporated grace.

# Chapter 2. and the Baptism in the New Testament.

For without substance no work can exist; therefore, they imagined or typified regeneration to themselves through the sacrifices by fire. In these sacrifices, they formed or envisioned the "Serpent-Destroyer" <note>A reference to Christ as the one who crushes the head of the serpent, as promised in Genesis 3:15.</note> within the fire. This typified how **He** would change God’s wrath-fire in the soul into a light-fire and love-fire; how the enmity would *separate* itself from the soul; and how the soul, through Christ’s death (in which the love of God would give itself up into this wrath-fire), should be changed into an angel.

By this modeled image or type, they pressed through the sacrifice to God with their desire and earnest prayer. God’s Word, along with His grace, had also modeled itself in men through the Covenant. Now there was a conjunction between God and man, for the human desire entered with this image through the sacrifice in the holy fire into God, and God’s love-desire entered into this image of the Serpent-Destroyer. For God kindled the sacrifice with the holy fire.

And it was not a common fire that they had in their sacrifices, though they used wood and offerings for it. Their fire did not come from a stone and a steel, but from the highest tincture <note>term: Tincture; in alchemy and Behmenist philosophy, this refers to a spiritual essence or active principle that can transform or "color" a substance.</note> of the paradisiacal ground from which the fire of life originated. If man could understand it, and did not continue to run on in blindness, it would be well for him, and he *would be* delivered from Babel <note>original: "Babell"; used here to represent spiritual confusion and false religious systems.</note> and fables.

*This* holy fire consumed their sacrifices through God’s imagination and kindling. There, the human will—which is introduced and clings to earthliness—is cleansed and purged in the holy fire, and ransomed from sin based on the future fulfillment <note>The "future fulfillment" refers to the coming of Christ, which the Old Testament sacrifices anticipated.</note>. For the ground out of which this holy fire came manifested itself *afterwards* in human life, in the person of Christ.

Thus the figure stood in their sacrifices to show how the earthly image of man should be preserved in the fire of God, and how the *grossness* of the elements should be consumed. Out of the consuming fire should go forth the right, true, created, pure, bright spiritual image originally in *Adam*, which—in the fire of God’s wrath—should be brought into clarity <note>original: "Clarity"; meaning spiritual brightness, purity, or transfiguration.</note> through this holy fire of great love. In this new image, the great fiery love would itself be the fire of life, so that it might no longer imagine falsehood.

14. With such an imagination and *belief* or faith, the first people before Christ’s time (before he manifested himself in this embodied Grace-Covenant and became man) were apprehended and taken into the Living Word of God. That is, they were taken into that grace wherein their soul entered into divine rest until the *fulfillment*, when Christ completed this process and arose from death. Then he also arose with his life and substance within them—specifically in their inward ground of the heavenly part which had vanished in *Adam*. Thus, they *have* put on Christ in regards to the soul and spiritual substance. They now wait for their body to be raised out of the *limus* <note>original: "limus"; Latin for "slime" or "mud," referring to the dust of the earth from which man was formed</note> of the earth—that is, the Third Principle, or the outspoken, formed, substantial *outward Word*—by his Spirit of the Resurrection at the Last Day. This is also how it is to be understood concerning Christians who *have* put on Christ here in this life.

<margin>Note.</margin>
15. This foundation of the *Holy Fire* began with *Adam* and *Abel*. When *Abel* and *Cain* offered sacrifices, God looked graciously on *Abel’s* sacrifice because the image or type of *Christ* stood within it through Abel's imagination of faith. Therefore, God kindled his sacrifice with the Holy Fire, and it was acceptable before him, for it was a conjunction with the Divine Desire. But God did not look graciously on *Cain’s* sacrifice, for he did not have *such* faith or *belief*. Instead, he stood in the lust and desire of his own perished nature and had imprinted or imagined for himself the kingdom of this world; therefore, the Holy Fire would not kindle in his image or type.

16. For *Cain* stood in the image of the perished *Adam*, as a true figure of *Adam* after the Fall. *Abel* stood in the figure of the New Regeneration—that is, in *Christ’s figure*—signifying how Christ would enter into death with his sacrifice. Thus, the image of the perished *Adam* stood near *Cain*, signifying how Christ had come to seek lost man and to generate him anew through his sacrifice.

17. But as the vanity of men gained the upper hand and their nature became increasingly evil and shameful, this understanding of the Holy Fire was quenched among them until the Flood came upon them and destroyed them. This was a type of Baptism, signifying how the water of Eternal Life (God’s substantial meekness) would drown and quench that false fire—the false life of the soul—and how the soul would grow up out of the Holy Water in the meekness of God into a *New Life* of Light.

# Chapter 2: And what Baptism is in the New Testament

With this faith, the first men before *Christ’s* time—before *Christ* manifested himself in this incorporated Covenant of Grace and became man—were apprehended and taken into the Living Word of God. That is, they were taken into the grace wherein their soul came into divine rest. <margin>Note: This remained the case until Christ fulfilled this type <note>original: "Type" - a prophetic symbol or foreshadowing</note> and arose from death.</margin> Then he also arose and was manifested with his life and substance in them—specifically, on their inward ground of the heavenly part which had vanished in *Adam*. Thus, they "put on *Christ*" in soul and spirit, and they now wait for their body to be formed out of the *limus* <note>original: "Limus" - Latin for slime or mud; here referring to the "dust of the ground" from which man was made</note> of the earth. This refers to the Third Principle <note>In the author's cosmology, the "Third Principle" is the visible, material world</note> of the visible world's property: the formed, outspoken, substantial Word of the Resurrection at the Last Day. This is also how it is to be understood concerning Christians today.

This ground of the *Holy Fire* began with *Adam*, and with *Abel* and *Cain*. When *Abel* and *Cain* sacrificed, God looked graciously on *Abel’s* sacrifice and kindled it with holy fire, *and the sweet smell went up before the LORD*. Because the image or type of *Christ* stood within *Abel's* faith, God kindled his sacrifice with the Holy Fire, and it was acceptable before God; it was a conjunction with the divine desire. But God did not look graciously on *Cain’s* sacrifice, for he did not have *such* faith or belief. Instead, *Cain* stood in the perished Adamic nature of his own lust and desire, and had imprinted or imagined to himself the kingdom of *this world*. Therefore, the Holy Fire would not kindle in his sacrifice.

*Cain* stood as a figure of the perished *Adam* after the Fall, and *Abel* stood as a figure of the New Regeneration. This signified how *Christ* would go into death with his sacrifice and die for man. The image of *Cain* stood nearby to signify how *Christ* had come to seek poor, fallen man and, through his sacrifice, generate him anew.

But as the vanity of men gained the upper hand and their nature became increasingly evil and shameful, this understanding of the Holy Fire was quenched among them until the Flood came and overthrew them. The Flood was a type of *Baptism*, signifying how the water of eternal life—namely, God’s substantial meekness—would drown and quench that false, fiery life of the soul. It showed how the soul would grow up out of the Holy Water in the meekness of God toward a *New Life* of Light.

# Chapter 2: What Baptism is in the New Testament

With this faith, the first people before Christ’s time—before Christ manifested himself in this incorporated Covenant of Grace and became man—were apprehended and taken into the Living Word of God. That is, they were taken into the Grace wherein their souls found divine rest. <note>The author argues that the faithful of the Old Testament were saved by the same Grace as Christians, though it was not yet fully revealed in the flesh.</note> They remained in this state until Christ fulfilled this type <term>type: a prophetic symbol or person representing a future reality</term> and arose from death. Then, he also arose and was manifested with his life and substance within them—specifically, on their inward ground of the heavenly part which had vanished in Adam. Thus, they "put on Christ" in soul and spirit. They now wait for their bodies to be raised from the *limus* <note>original: *limus*; Latin for "mud" or "slime," referring to the dust of the earth from which man was made</note> of the earth—that is, the third principle of the visible world’s property, the formed, outspoken, substantial Word of the Resurrection at the Last Day. This is also how it should be understood concerning Christians today.

This ground of the **Holy Fire** began with Adam, and with Abel and Cain. When Abel and Cain sacrificed, God looked graciously on Abel’s sacrifice and kindled it with Holy Fire, *and the sweet smell ascended before the LORD.* Because the image or type of Christ stood within Abel's faith, God kindled his sacrifice with the Holy Fire, and it was acceptable before Him; it was a conjunction with the divine desire. But God did not look graciously on Cain’s sacrifice, for he did not have *such* faith. Instead, Cain stood in the perished Adamic nature of his own lust and desire, and had imprinted or imagined within himself the kingdom of *this world*. Therefore, the Holy Fire would not kindle in his sacrifice.

Cain stood as a figure of the perished Adam after the Fall, and Abel stood as a figure of the New Regeneration. This signified how Christ would go into death with his sacrifice and die for man. The image of Cain stood nearby to signify how Christ had come to seek poor, fallen man and, through his sacrifice, generate him anew.

But as the vanity of men gained the upper hand, and their nature became increasingly evil and shameful, this understanding of the Holy Fire was quenched among them. Eventually, the Flood came upon them and overthrew them. This was a type of **Baptism**, signifying how the water of eternal life—namely, God’s substantial meekness—would drown and quench that false, fiery life of the soul, and how the soul would grow up out of the Holy Water in the meekness of God to a **New Life** of Light.

Now, when the time came that God renewed his Covenant with Abraham—the same covenant He had established in Paradise—He gave him the figure of Christ again through Circumcision and the Holy Fire.

# Chapter 2. and the Baptism in the New Testament.

Fire; as is to be seen by the sacrifices of *Abraham* in Genesis 15: how the fire came forth between the parts, and how at the same time in a vision, terror and great anguish fell upon him. All of this signifies the death of Christ and the transmutation <note>original: "transmutation"; the spiritual transformation or changing of one substance into another</note> of souls.

The circumcision of the organ of human propagation was a figure <note>original: "figure"; a symbolic representation or type</note> signifying how the fleshly man—born of masculine and feminine seed—should be cut off from the image created in *Adam* through the death of Christ and the anger of God. Therefore, God placed his Covenant of Grace in the figure of Christ upon this member, and so set Christ before Him—whom He had established as a throne of grace—so that they might walk before Him, and *that His anger might not consume them* (Genesis 17).

And He sets forth the figure of Christ and His process through the example of *Isaac*, signifying how the redemption of mankind should be effected: how God’s love-fire should swallow up God’s wrath-fire within *man* and turn it into love.

22. Since these beast-like properties remained attached to the mind of man—namely, the animal soul influenced by the stars—their prayers and will were not pure before God. Therefore, God’s wrath-fire consumed this beast-like vanity of man within the sacrifice through those animal properties. Their modeled image or type of grace ascended with their prayer into the *holy fire*. There, the foolish desire was received; that same holy fire then pierced through the Father’s wrath-fire in the sacrifice, carrying the received human desire with it.

<margin>Atoned.</margin>
23. In this way, the children of Israel were <gloss>atoned or released</gloss> from their sins and vanity in a spiritual manner through the sacrifice and fire. This was based upon the *future fulfillment*, until Christ would come and take on our humanity. He gave himself up to God his Father as a sacrifice into his *wrath-fire*, and with the manifested source of love in the name JESUS, he turned that wrath into love. Then, the wrath swallowed up the human self-will, and God’s love-will—through Christ’s love—grew forth through death and through the wrath. Thus, humanity passed through *death* into eternal life.

24. In this manner, Israel was ransomed from *sins* in a spiritual way through the type <note>original: "Type"; a prophetic symbol or foreshadowing</note> of Christ, through the covenant of God, and through the sacrifice and fire. Israel’s faith passed through the sacrifice into the *covenant* of God—specifically into the grace-atonement of the "seed of the woman" <note>A reference to the promise in Genesis 3:15</note>. God’s imagination also entered into his covenant made with *Adam* and *Abraham*, and there a conjunction occurred. The true atonement in the covenant was accomplished through the holy fire. This holy fire later manifested itself in the humanity of *Christ*, taking away the strength from the poison of God’s *wrath* and turning it into love. It broke open the strong grip <note>original: "Impression"</note> of death in the soul-fire and brought divine love and meekness into it.

25. But when the time drew near that God would manifest his love-fire through his covenant in humanity—so that this same fire should be kindled in human life—God *renewed* the type. With Saint John, he began the *water baptism* and moved his covenant from circumcision into baptism.

->26. *But Reason asks:*<-
->*What is or what does water baptism signify?*<-
->*What does God do through it? Also,*<-
->*what does it effect?*<-

<margin>Text: Imprinted or compressed.</margin>
Answer: As mentioned above, the soul had so harshly <gloss>compressed or imprinted</gloss> itself in its properties within the wrathful *anger of God* that it stood in eternal death. In the soul, there was no longer any divine *love-essence* <note>original: "Ens"; the essential substance or spiritual being</note> manifest or active through which it might be able to kindle the holy fire of the *love of God*. Therefore, God sent baptism before this holy kindling and placed it in the first covenant. For when the Word and the power of the holy fire became man and manifested in Christ, the Holy Word in the holy fire—through the assumed humanity—spoke within itself to its fellow members <note>i.e., other human beings</note> according to that humanity.

<margin>Soaking, steeping, or immersion.</margin>
27. Now, if this "inspeaking" <note>The divine act of speaking the Word into the soul</note> was to take effect in man and become substantial, then the divine <gloss>infusion or soaking</gloss> had to come first. For just as the *Word* in the covenant manifested itself in humanity, then—

# Chapter 2. and the Baptism in the New Testament.

And thereupon God gave him the promise, *that this promised incorporated Seed*, which would—through the transmutation in the fire and through the dying of vanity—grow forth in the love-fire, *would be as great as the stars in heaven.*

But when the time drew near that God would manifest his love-fire through his covenant in humanity, so that the same fire should be kindled in human life, he then brought his covenant (which had been symbolized by circumcision) into Water Baptism; and with Saint John, the *Water Baptism* began.

->*But Reason asks:*<-
->*What is or what does the Water Baptism signify?*<-

Answer: As mentioned above, the soul had so enraged itself with wrath in its own properties that *thereby* it stood in eternal death. In the soul, God’s *love-essence* <note>original: "Love-Effence"; refers to the divine substance or being of God's love</note> was no longer manifest, in which it might be able to kindle the holy fire of God—**namely**, the holy life. Therefore, God sent the holy baptism beforehand and placed it within the covenant. For when the Word—**namely**, the power of the holy fire—became man and revealed itself in Christ, then Christ, with the holy fire through his assumed humanity, spoke himself into his fellow members <note>The "fellow members" refers to the rest of humanity as part of the body of Christ</note> according to his human nature.

Now, if this "in-speaking" <note>The act of God speaking His Word into the human soul</note> was to take effect and become *substantial*, then the divine <margin>x Immersion or Infusion <note>original: "Eintauchen"</note></margin> x infusion must go before it. For as soon as the Word manifested itself in humanity, then the meek love and grace...

# What the Sacrifices and Circumcision [Signified] in the Old Testament. Chapter 2.

The meek love and grace in the Covenant flowed forth: with this 
<margin>Instituted.</margin>
flowing forth of the Divine Love, the Covenant was established and placed in Water-Baptism. Because man was earthly and made of the elements, there had to be an elementary *medium* or means for that purpose, through which the flowing forth of the Divine Love in the Covenant might take hold. This allowed a human, natural substance to be in the middle, where both the *Divine* and the *human imagination* <note>original: "Imagination"; here meaning the focused desire, will, or creative power of the mind</note> might enter and infuse itself into humanity as a new *essence* <note>original: "Ens"</note> or kindler, in which the Holy Fire might ignite itself within the dry fire of the soul.

28. This was done under circumcision and in the sacrifices; there, the animal fat was a medium. Through such kindling, the human desire—acting through the Covenant—went to meet God, and God’s imagination entered into the Covenant. Thus, the Holy Fire stood in the Covenant, positioned opposite to the Wrath during the introduction of human desire in the sacrifice. God’s imagination in his love-fire entered the Covenant, and the human imagination also passed through the sacrifice in the fire into the Covenant. In the fire, God’s *wrath* was encountered; through this, the human imagination had to pass into the Covenant to consume the sin and uncleanness in the human desire that reached toward God. Thus, in the Covenant, the *love*-fire stood opposite to the human desire, and it drew the human desire out of the wrath-fire to itself, according to its purity.

29. This may be seen by *Moses* on *Mount Sinai*, where first the Father’s property in the Covenant opened itself out of the wrath-fire and required man’s righteousness—demanding that man should walk before God in full obedience and purity, and in purity draw near to him through the sacrifice in the fire. If not, then he would devour them in the curse through this wrath-fire. This was an image, type, or shadow signifying how human vanity should and must be purged through God’s wrath-fire. But in that same wrath-fire, the love-fire stood opposite in the Covenant within the sacrifice, as an image of Christ, and quenched the wrath-fire. This allowed the soul’s desire, with its prayer and will, to pierce through this wrath-fire into God.

30. This love-fire, which stood opposite to the wrath in the sacrifice and was also kindled by the wrath-fire so that it devoured the sacrifice, is that which awakened itself with heavenly substantiality within the limit of the Covenant in the "seed of the woman." That is, it awakened with the essential wisdom and gave itself up into our substance—which had vanished in relation to God, yet remained *heavenly*.

31. Our substance, which had vanished and was shut up in death, was signified by the dry rod of *Aaron*. This substance grew again through this awakening and the introduction of the 
<margin>Or, Substance.</margin>
heavenly *living love-essence*, where God’s substance became man, in whom the Holy Fire could burn. For the Divine essence <note>original: "Ens"</note> which vanished in *Adam* grew again with such kindling; it was the food of this love-fire, like a spiritual *Holy Oil* in which the love-fire could kindle and burn. That same love-burning was the new life of the regeneration.

<margin>By "OTHER" may be meant other men besides the man Christ himself.</margin>
32. But since in other *men* such a supernatural kindling should not be effected through a special moving of God—but rather that all other men should be kindled through this holy fire coming out of Christ—therefore went the...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the spiritual mechanics of the Covenant and how divine grace interacts with human nature through physical elements.</meta>

In the Covenant, the divine love "flowed forth." With this flowing forth of divine love in the Covenant, the Covenant was established in water baptism. Since man was of an elementary, earthly nature, there also had to be an *elementary medium* <note>original: "Elementary Medium" — a physical substance acting as a bridge between the spiritual and material worlds</note> or means for that purpose, through which the flowing forth of the divine love in the Covenant might take hold. This allowed a human, natural substance to be in the middle, where the *divine and human imagination might enter* and <margin>$\gamma$ Immerse or infuse itself.</margin> $\gamma$ infuse itself into humanity as an *ens* <note>original: "Ens" — a philosophical term for an essential being or "seed" of existence</note> or kindler, in which the holy fire might ignite itself within the dry fire of the soul.

This was the same process used in circumcision and in the sacrifices, where the animal *fat* was a medium. In those acts, the human desire—through such kindling with the Covenant—went to meet God, and God’s imagination similarly went to meet the Covenant in man. Thus, the holy fire stood opposite to the divine wrath and consumed the uncleanness in the human will, so that the will might pierce through to God.

We see this same image through *Moses* on *Mount Sinai*. There, first, the Father’s property was manifested in the fire, requiring man’s full obedience to live before God in holiness and to draw near to Him in purity through sacrifice. If they did not, He would devour them in the curse through this fire. This was also an image, type, or shadow signifying how human vanity should and must be purged through God’s wrath-fire. However, *Israel* could not reach salvation and the love of God through the fire, nor could they enter the Promised Land through *Moses*; they could only do so through *Joshua* and *Christ*. <note>The author draws a parallel between Joshua leading Israel into Canaan and Jesus (whose name is the same in Hebrew) leading humanity into spiritual salvation.</note>

Therefore, God brought His heavenly substance with His Covenant into water baptism, so that there might

Covenant through the *Water-Baptism* with the outflowing Grace first into the Heavenly *Ens* <note>original: *Ens*; a philosophical term for "being" or "essence"</note> of Man—namely, into the vanished substance where the incorporated Paradisiacal Covenant of the Serpent-Destroyer <note>A reference to Christ as the one who crushes the head of the serpent (Genesis 3:15).</note> *stood*—and pierced into that same Covenant.

33. Yet seeing that the human essence, which had become earthly, should go together with such a piercing in, there must also be a *medium* for it, in which the human essence *can* take hold. For it *cannot* take hold in God’s holiness, because the human will was torn away from that; therefore, there must now be a *similitude* <note>A physical representation or likeness that corresponds to a spiritual reality.</note> in which the imagination of human nature may take hold.

34. For Christ also had this similitude—namely, the elements received from us humans—so that the Divine Imagination might take hold in the *water*. In this way, the Divine and human imagination together might awaken and kindle the incorporated Paradisiacal Covenant, in the same manner as a glimmering (*mother*) fire or tinder-fire enters the wood and begins to glow.

35. In such a manner, through baptism, a Divine "mother-fire" of the Holy Fire was introduced (a holy and Divine glimmering fire). This destroyed and broke *sin* and *death* to pieces in the inwardness of man—namely, in the vanished heavenly *essence*. From this, the dry, withered tree of the inward ground *might* receive life again—namely, a spiritual *oil*. In this new spiritual oil of the *power* of the Divine Light, the love-fire (the New Life) should burn.

36. <note>The original text repeats the number 35 in error; it is modernized here as 36 for clarity of sequence.</note> This is now the *Water-Baptism*, for which the Holy Spirit in the inward ground is the hand. With the flowing forth of Divine Love out of Christ’s suffering, death, and resurrection, the Spirit baptizes with His victory. That is, He infuses Christ’s humanity, suffering, death, and resurrection into the inward ground, and kindles the incorporated Paradisiacal Covenant with this fire, so that the dry "Rod of Aaron" <note>A biblical reference to Aaron’s rod miraculously budding (Numbers 17), symbolizing life coming from death or spiritual rebirth.</note> buds forth.

37. For with this infusion of the Holy Spirit, Christ is bestowed upon man. Man is hereby incorporated into Christ; and the heavenly *essence*, which in *Mary* assumed our human *essence*, is put on him along with the whole process of Christ. It is imprinted in his own heavenly vanished *essence* toward a New Life which *has* overcome death.

38. As a *tincture* <note>In alchemy and early chemistry, a substance that can infuse and transform the properties of another material.</note> completely tints and thoroughly penetrates metal, or as fire glows all the way through iron, so it is to be understood here concerning those who are capable of such infusion, as shall be further taught.

# Chapter 2: And what the Baptism in the New Testament is

...might be a tolerable *medium* <note>original: "Medium"; a middle ground or vehicle</note>, whereby he might introduce his love-essence into our vanished and heavenly substance, wherein the holy fire might kindle itself again.

This was done specifically because the human essence had become *earthly*. In order for man to bear or endure it, there had to be such a *medium* that the human essence could grasp. For without a *medium*, the human essence cannot take hold of God’s holiness; the human will had been torn away from it.

Therefore, God became man so that he might cause his *deity to flow into us along with his humanity*, so that we might comprehend or take hold of him. This was also so that the divine imagination <note>The creative, formative power of God's mind</note> might introduce and awaken itself in man and work together with the human imagination. Therefore, he assumed humanity so that he might work in us with the deity through the humanity.

Thus, with the water-baptism, a glimmering, mother-holy-fire was *imprinted in God’s love as a subject* or object of divine imagination and work. Through this, death—according to the *inwardness* of man—would be broken to pieces and a new life would spring forth. Just as fire begins to glimmer in wood, so was this *infusion* or baptizing an <gloss>anointing</gloss> <margin>Or *unction*.</margin> of the Holy Spirit, serving as a new oil of life in which the divine light might kindle itself.

This is now the *basis* of *water baptism*, for which the Holy Spirit in the inward ground is the *hand*. With the flowing forth of divine love out of Christ’s suffering, death, and resurrection, the Spirit baptizes with his victory. That is: he infuses Christ’s humanity, suffering, death, and resurrection into man. He kindles the incorporated paradisical covenant with this fire, so that the dry rod of Aaron <note>A biblical reference to Numbers 17, symbolizing miraculous life springing from what was dead</note> begins to bud forth again.

For with this infusion of the Holy Spirit, Christ is *bestowed* upon man. Man is hereby incorporated into Christ; and the heavenly *essence* <note>original: "Ens"; the fundamental being or spiritual substance</note>, which in the Virgin *Mary* assumed our human *essence*, is put on and imprinted in him for a new life, along with the entire process of Christ's work.

As a *tincture* <note>In alchemy, a substance capable of transforming or "coloring" the nature of another</note> transforms a *metal*, or as fire glows through iron, so it is to be understood here concerning those who are capable of receiving this infusion, as will be explained further.

### The Third Chapter.

## A briefer and *more* fundamental instruction on how man is baptized by the *Holy Spirit*, through *Christ’s* suffering, death, and resurrection in both body and soul.

->1.<-

When God intended to transition his covenant from circumcision into *Water-Baptism*, the Word of "Inspoken Grace" <note>original: "Inspoken Grace"; refers to the divine promise or "breath" of grace spoken into humanity after the Fall</note>—which contained the holy fire of God—first became a man. He took on the "woman's seed" <note>A biblical reference to the humanity of Christ born of Mary</note> as *our* soul and humanity, so that he *might* baptize us with the Living Covenant that had now become human.

2. For the body of man, for which baptism was *necessary*, was made out of the elements. Therefore, if the body was to be baptized, the Covenant had to first place itself into an elementary *medium* <note>A middle-ground or vehicle</note>, as it did in the humanity of Christ. He had to sanctify that *medium* so that man could be baptized through it.

3. This was not done *only* for the heavenly substance of man which vanished in Adam—the substance in which the Covenant in Paradise originally established itself—so that it *alone* should be baptized. No; it was also for the soul and for the body made from the *slime* <note>original: "Limus"; the dust or clay of the earth from which Adam was formed</note> of the earth.

4. The *whole* man needed baptism. All three principles of all *three* worlds within man had to be baptized: 
*   The *first* principle is the Eternal Nature—the true life of the soul—which came out of the "Inspoken Word" into the body. 
*   The *second* principle is the true Eternal Spirit—the holy power of light and love (which I call in this little book the heavenly vanished *essence* <note>original: "Ens"</note> and substance in *Adam*). This is where Adam vanished; that is, the soul's will departed from it. 
*   The *third* principle is the astral animal soul <note>The part of human nature governed by the stars and physical instincts</note>,

### The Third Chapter.

# A Briefer and *more* fundamental Instruction, how Man is Baptized by the *Holy Ghost*, with Christ’s Suffering, Death, and Resurrection, in Body and Soul.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'W' featuring floral and vine motifs within a square border.</image-desc>

When God intended to introduce his Covenant with *Water Baptism* into humanity, the "in-spoken" Word of Grace of God’s holy fire—namely, the fiery Love—had first become Man. He first took the woman’s seed <note>A reference to the "seed of the woman" from Genesis 3:15, interpreted as the humanity of Christ.</note> upon himself—namely, *our soul*, spirit, and whole humanity—so that *he might* baptize us with the living Covenant, which was both God and Man.

For the body of man, for which baptism was necessary, was made out of the elements. Now, if it were to be baptized, the Covenant would first give itself into an elementary *medium* <note>original: "Medium"; here meaning a physical means or channel.</note>, as it did in the humanity of Christ, and sanctify that *medium*, so that man might be baptized through it.

For this was not to be done *only* for the heavenly substance which vanished in *Paradise*. In that substance—the most inward ground of humanity—the Covenant in Paradise incorporated itself. It was not that this ground *alone* should be baptized; no, it was also for the soul, and for the body made from the *slime* <note>original: "Limus"; the dust or clay of the earth from which Adam was formed.</note> of the earth.

The *whole* man needs baptism. All the Three Principles—that is, the properties of all *three worlds* in man—must be baptized. 

1. *The first Principle* is the Eternal Nature, the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"; in Boehme's theosophy, the chaos or potentiality from which all things arise.</note>, out of which the visible world has sprung forth; it is the ground of the true Eternal Soul which came into the body through God’s breathing into it. 
2. *The second Principle* is the true Eternal Spirit, namely, the Holy Light’s Power. (In this little book, I call this power the heavenly *essence* <note>original: "Ens"; the spiritual being or substance.</note> or substance that vanished in *Adam*). This vanished in *Adam* at the Fall, as the soul’s will departed from it into earthliness,

# How Man is Baptized Ch. 3.

The soul, from the spirit of the world <note>original: *Ex Spiritu Mundi*</note>, with its body out of the slime <note>original: *Limus*; referring to the dust or clay of the earth</note> of the earth—namely, the whole outward, visible man.

5. This threefold man was wholly fallen; for as the divine light in the spirit of the second principle <note>In the author's cosmology, the "Second Principle" represents the divine world of light and love</note> went out, he was quite blind regarding God and dead regarding Paradise. Into this man, a divine love-substance <note>original: *Ens*; a philosophical term for the essential "being" or "essence" of a thing</note> must be infused again. In this love-substance, the divine fire and light might again kindle to a new life. But if this is to be done, then the holy fire must first manifest itself with the Covenant in the threefold humanity—as it did in Christ’s humanity—so that God’s Spirit might baptize us out of, with, and *through* this threefold humanity; so that each principle within us might be baptized with its own likeness or similarity.

->*For the Holy Spirit baptizes through Christ for the forgiveness of sins.*<-

6. The holy fire of the divine power in Christ baptizes his temple in *us*, which the holy fire—namely, the divine life in *us*—would possess. This includes the vanished substance <note>original: *Ens*</note> of the heavenly world’s essence, the spirit of understanding or of power (the second principle), or the angelic body out of the angelic world’s substance. To this substance, Christ afterwards gives his most holy spiritual flesh for food, in which he himself dwells. This divine love in the holy fire baptizes this spirit, for it is a substance of the holy fire wherein it burns or lives. And the first principle—namely, the fiery soul out of the divine knowledge <note>original: *Science*</note> of the separable, speaking Word, out of the Father’s property—is baptized with the fiery spirit of the Father’s property, as with the fire of burning love.

7. ->*To be understood in this way:*<-

The soul is of the Father’s property, and in this baptism, by his infusion into the soul, he gives it to the Son in his love-fire. The Father’s property in the fire takes hold of the soul, first with the law of nature, with his strict righteousness, and with the eternal <margin>contraction or compression</margin> birth of the fire-ground. By this, the hard <gloss>compressed</gloss> dead soul is made movable in that infusion of the fire, and its hard <gloss>compressed</gloss> compression...

# Chapter 3. By the Holy Ghost, etc.

ness, and broke its will off from it. *The Third Principle* is the man from the outward world's substance—namely, the astral soul <note>The "astral soul" refers to the psychological and emotional nature governed by the stars or cosmos.</note> with its body made out of the *limus* <note>original: "Limus"; Latin for "mud" or "slime," referring to the dust of the ground in Genesis.</note> of the earth, which consists of the four elements.

This *threefold man* was completely fallen; for as soon as the light in the spirit of the second principle went out, he was quite blind toward God and dead toward Paradise. Into this, a divine love-essence <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for the fundamental essence or "being" of a thing.</note> must be infused or imprinted again, wherein the divine fire and light might kindle itself to a new life. Therefore, the holy fire of the *great love of God* in the Covenant manifested itself in the humanity of Christ, so that God’s Spirit might baptize us out of, with, and through this threefold humanity; so that each principle in us might be baptized with its like or similarity. For the *Holy Ghost baptizes through Christ for the forgiveness of sins.*

->*How is this baptism of the Holy Ghost effected?*<-

*Answer:* God’s holy fire of the divine love-power in Christ Jesus baptizes His temple in *us*, which the holy fire-life—namely, God’s Spirit—will possess. This includes the vanished essence of the heavenly world's substance, the spirit of power and understanding, and the second principle or angelic ground. To this ground, Christ afterwards gives His holy spiritual flesh for food, in which the true <margin>b His own very reflected image.</margin> express image of God consists. The divine love and sweetness in the holy fire baptizes this spirit, for it is an essence of the holy fire wherein it burns or lives. And the first principle—namely, the fiery soul out of the divine knowledge of the separable, eternal-speaking Word out of the Father’s fiery substance—is baptized with the fiery spirit of the Father’s property, as with the fire-burning love.

->*Understand it in this way:*<-

The soul is of the Father’s property, according to the fiery omnipotence; and in this baptism, in which the Father co-baptizes, *He gives it to the Son* in His love-fire. Understand that the soul is of the Father’s fire, but it has become a wrath-fire. Through this infusion, He gives this wrath-fire to His fire-burning love. The Father’s property in the fire takes hold of the soul, first with the *Law of Nature*, as with His strict righteousness of the eternal birth of the fire-ground. By this, the hard <margin>c Imprinted or compressed. d Immersion or dipping.</margin> compressed, dead soul—in that same immersion of the fire of the divine life—is made movable. Its hard compression of the false magnetic desire is broken apart and opened, in that man—

<summary>This page explains the "threefold" nature of humanity (elemental, astral, and divine) and how the Holy Spirit uses the "fire of divine love" to restore the fallen soul to its original image through Christ.</summary>

# How Man is Baptized Ch. 3.

of the false magnetic desire <note>original: "Magnetick Desire"; referring to the soul's selfish or worldly attraction</note> is broken asunder and opened, in the same manner as a man strikes a flint to produce fire.

8. Thus now the Divine Light of the Great Love is present in the opened name **JESUS**, which comes out of **JEHOVAH** in the center of the Father’s fire. When the Father’s fire unlocks the fiery soul, then the light of Divine Love can shine into it and introduce the Love *essence* <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for the fundamental nature or substance of a thing</note> into it. For as soon as the wrathful fire unlocks the soul, it *instantly* receives the love-fire of the Son and enters into it, just as fire glows through iron, or as a *tincture* <note>original: "Tincture"; in alchemy, a substance that can infuse and transform the properties of another</note> penetrates metal.

9. And in this way, the soul is *given* from the Father to the Son, and thus the Son *gives* it the love-fire—that is, Eternal Life. For if the Father’s property did not also move within the shut-up soul, then the soul would remain in its own compression in eternal death, and the Light *could not* be manifested within it.

<margin>
Commotion or contrition.
</margin>
10. The shrill sounding <note>original: "shril sounding"; likely referring to a sudden, piercing spiritual awakening or vibration</note> and kindling of the Father’s fire is the fountain of human repentance, where man trembles before sin. For in that flash of fire, the false, compressed vanity of *sins* in the darkness of the soul is made manifest; and the inward shining love of the Son is the meek "Love-Oil" that unites and tempers that *contrition* or piercing sound once again.

11. *For the Son is in the Father, and the Father is in the Son, and they baptize together: the Father with fire, and the Son with Light,* in the same way that fire is struck so that the meek light-glance is revealed.

# Chapter 3. By the Holy Ghost.

In the same manner as a man strikes a flint to produce fire, a new fire of God is kindled. <margin>Trembling or contrition.</margin> This commotion, or "shrill sounding" <note>original: *concussion*; referring to a spiritual vibration or awakening</note>, is also the foundation of repentance.

Now, when the Father’s fire is stirred and kindled, the Divine Light of the great, sweet Love shines forth in the revealed name **JESUS** out of the Holy Name **JEHOVAH**. This occurs in the center of the Father’s fire, arising from the unity of God as the beams of Divine Grace in the soul. For the soul is unlocked by this glimpse of light; then, suddenly, the Light of Love takes hold of the hidden ground of the soul and fills it with the substance of Love.

And thus the Son—that is, the Love of the Father—receives the soul, sanctifies it, and thoroughly penetrates it, just as fire thoroughly penetrates iron. And that is what Christ meant when he said: *“Father, the men were yours, and you have given them to me; and I give them eternal life. Also: This is eternal life, that they truly know you, Father, that you are the true God, and Jesus Christ, whom you have sent.”* (John 17:3, 6).
<margin>Note.</margin>

In this indwelling and inward-shining Light, the New Birth occurs, and God is understood and known in the Spirit. Immediately following this is true Faith, which desires nothing except what God desires. In this way, Christ is the *Light of the Microcosm* <note>original: *Light of the Little World*; a common Renaissance term for man as a reflection of the universe</note>, and he gives the eternal Light-Life of God to the soul.

Thus, the soul lives in the Father, yet is governed and sanctified through the office of the Son. Its anxious "fire-life" <note>the soul's natural, restless energy</note> becomes a pure burning of love. However, the Father’s "concussion" or "shrill sounding" is necessary so that the source of the soul's fire may be touched, allowing the soul to realize its true nature. By this, the soul is brought into continual repentance and humility. When the beam of God’s wrath <note>the corrective or purifying aspect of God's power</note> frequently touches it, the soul considers its fall and is never overconfident. It recognizes it has a great enemy nearby in its own flesh and blood—namely, Satan’s poison—so that it may no longer follow its own selfish will as it did before. For the soul, standing in this beam of wrath, trembles before sin, and so sin is...

It emphasizes that spiritual trembling and the recognition of "Satan's poison" in the flesh are necessary for maintaining humility and repentance.</summary>

# Chapter 3. By the Holy Ghost.

In the same manner as a man strikes a flint to produce fire, a new fire of God is kindled. 
<margin>Trembling or contrition.</margin>
This **commotion**, or resonant vibration, is also the foundation of repentance.

Now, when the Father’s fire is stirred and kindled, the Divine Light of the great, sweet Love shines forth. This occurs in the revealed name JESUS, emerging from the Holy Name JEHOVAH <note>original: JEHOVA; the author uses these names to represent the transition from the stern Law/Justice of the Father to the Mercy/Grace of the Son</note> in the *center* of the Father’s fire. It comes out of the unity of God as the beams of Divine Grace into the soul. With this glimpse, the soul is unlocked, and suddenly the Light of Love takes hold of the previously hidden depths and fills them with the substance of Love.

And thus the Son—that is, the Love of the Father—receives the soul, sanctifies it, and thoroughly penetrates it, just as fire thoroughly penetrates iron. And that is what Christ meant when he said: *“Father, the men were yours, and you have given them to me; and I give them eternal life.”* Also: *“This is eternal life: that they may rightly know you, Father, that you are the true God, and Jesus Christ, whom you have sent”* (John 17:3, 6).
<margin>Note.</margin>

In this indwelling and inward-shining Light, the New Birth occurs, and God is understood and known in the Spirit. Immediately following this is true Faith, which desires nothing except what God desires. In this way, Christ is the Light of the "Little World" <note>original: Microcosm; a common Renaissance term for Man as a reflection of the universe</note>—that is, of Man—and gives the eternal Light-Life of God to the soul.

Thus, the soul lives in the Father, yet is governed and sanctified through the office of the Son. Its anxious "fire-life" <note>the soul's natural state of restless desire</note> becomes a pure burning of love. However, the Father’s *concussion* <note>original: Concussion; a jarring or shaking of the spirit</note> or resonant sounding is necessary so that the source of the soul's fire may be touched, allowing the soul to realize its true condition. By this, the soul is brought into continual *repentance* and humility. When the beam of God’s wrath frequently touches it, the soul considers its fall and is never overconfident. It has a great enemy nearby in its own flesh and blood—namely, Satan’s poison—and so it must no longer follow its own will as it did before. For the soul, standing in this beam of wrath, trembles before sin, and so sin is manifested...

It emphasizes that spiritual trembling and the recognition of sin are necessary for maintaining humility and faith.</summary>

For where the self-will of sin is *shaken* through repentance, there the holy anointing oil of Love penetrates instantly and heals these wounds. Thus the Father baptizes with fire to repentance, and the Son with Love to sanctification; and the Holy Ghost manages the office, who baptizes with a new life. For the whole Holy Trinity baptizes according to the Divine Manifestation. For without and beyond the manifestation, there is only one Triune God in one only good **being** <margin>Substance or Essence.</margin> and will, of whom a man cannot say: "the Father is wrath," or this or that, but is the Triune Good Being. In which fire, the bitter suffering and dying of **Jesus Christ** is impressed into the poor soul; for the wrath-fire which holds the soul captive is overcome and softened with Love. And this is impressed in the soul as a *victory*; and the Son baptizes with the holy anointing oil of God’s Love, and heals the poor shaken soul again. And the true Comforter, the Holy Ghost—who goes forth through Christ’s death, through Christ’s resurrection, through the Father in the Son—he baptizes with a *new life*, and gives true faith and understanding, so that we receive and know this.

# How Man is Baptized: Chapter 3

12. ...considered: By the *Water*—namely, by the element of the Body of Christ—the true Adamic man <note>original: "Adamicall Man"; referring to the human nature created in Adam</note> is baptized. This man was created in Adam regarding the body, formed out of the substance of the outward world. Here the Holy Spirit baptizes, who proceeds from the Father and the Son. His proceeding is the formation of the world; the world is the "outspoken," formed Word, and it is *the Spirit of God* that has formed it.

13. For He is to be understood as existing in all *three worlds*, acting in each world according to its specific property. In the Father’s wrath, according to the *Darkness*, He is the flame of *painfulness*. In the Eternal Light, He is the *Love-flame of God*. And in this world, in the *Spirit of the World* <note>original: "Spiritu Mundi"</note>, He is the *shaper* and *master-craftsman* of every thing, acting in every thing according to its nature. As the "Separator" <note>The internal power that divides and distinguishes qualities in a substance</note> of a thing is, so also is the outflowing Spirit from the "outspoken" Word within that thing.

14. Regarding the substance of the outward world, it should not be understood that the spirit of its substance *is* called *God* itself; rather, it is the outflowing Spirit within the "outspoken" Word of God, which has its foundation in the Word of God.

15. The outflowing Spirit of the outward creaturely and natural life has flowed forth out of God’s Love and Wrath, out of *Light* and *Darkness*—namely, out of the first and second Principles <note>The "Principles" are Boehme's terms for the dark/fire world and the light/love world</note>—as well as out of Eternal Nature and the *Speaking* of the Word. It stands with its foundation in God’s Speaking, for the Eternal Word <margin>Or, breathes forth that outspoken Spirit.</margin> <gloss>breathes</gloss> itself out with that Spirit into a creaturely life.

16. That same exhaled Spirit is the *outward* creaturely life in every creature, according to its specific nature. It is the Soul of the *Outward* World—the beginning soul, a life made of the four elements.

Its power is a fiery and light-filled *Constellation* <note>The "Constellation" refers to the internal astral or governing influence on a person's nature</note>. Whatever the entire outward Constellation is in its own power, this Spirit is the same within itself. Yet, it exists as a "shut up" Constellation that lies in the *Temperament* <note>The balanced state of the elements</note>, and yet it unfolds itself in every life and makes itself distinct according to that life’s property.

<margin>Text: By.</margin> <gloss>By</gloss> In temporary creatures, it acts with a temporary property; <gloss>and</gloss> in eternal creatures, it acts with an eternal property.

In Man, it acts with a temporary and fragile, corruptible property, and also with an eternal one.

This *eternal* part is attached to the Eternal Exhaled Word (to the Eternal Exhaling). Because of this, Man shall arise and come again on the *Last Day* in his visible image, leaving behind corruptibility. He shall be presented before God’s <margin>Or, fiery trial.</margin> Judgment and be put to the great <gloss>fiery trial</gloss> <note>original: "fires-Proba"; a test by fire</note>, where the mortal part shall separate itself from the eternal.

<summary>This page explains the role of the Holy Spirit as the "master-craftsman" of the physical and spiritual worlds, describing how the "outspoken Word" of God gives life to all creatures and prepares man for the final judgment.</summary>
<keywords>Holy Spirit, Three Worlds, Spiritus Mundi, Adamic Man, Outspoken Word, Constellation, Last Day, Fire-Proba, Baptism</keywords>

--- Page 86 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the mystical nature of Baptism and the role of the Holy Spirit in the creation and sustenance of the world.</meta>

By the *Water*—namely, by the element of the body of Christ, the true Adamic man <note>original: "Adamicall Man"; referring to the perfected human state before the Fall</note> *out of the elements* which was created in Adam—understand that the substance of the outward world is baptized. For here the Holy Spirit baptizes, who proceeds from the Father and the Son. His proceeding is the formation of the world, and the world is the "outspoken Word"; it is the Spirit of God that has formed that *outspoken Word*.

He is to be understood as existing in all *Three Principles* or worlds, acting in each world according to its specific property. **First**, in the Father’s wrath according to the Darkness, He is the flame of *painfulness*. **Secondly**, in the Eternal Light, He is the *love-flame* of God. And **Thirdly**, in this world, in the *Spirit of the World* <note>original: "Spiritu Mundi"</note>, He is the *former* and master-worker of everything. He works in everything according to its own property; as the separator or *formative power* <note>original: "Archæus"; a Paracelsian term for the vital force that directs growth and healing</note> of a thing is, from which it arises, so also is the outflowing Spirit from the outspoken Word present in everything.

And it is *not* to be understood that the spirit of the outward substance is itself called *God*; rather, it is the outflowing Spirit in the outspoken Word of God, which stands with its foundation in the Word of God.

The *outflowing Spirit* of the outward natural and creaturely life has flowed forth out of God’s love and wrath, out of *Light* and *Darkness*—namely, out of the first and second Principles, out of Eternal Nature, and out of the *speaking* of the Word. For the Eternal Word has breathed itself with that Spirit into a creaturely, image-like life.

It is the soul of the *outward world*, a life of the four elements. Its power is like a fiery and light-filled *constellation*. Whatever the whole outward constellation is in itself in its power, that same Spirit is everywhere alone in itself; yet it exists as a hidden constellation that lies in the *balanced temperament* <note>original: "Temperament"; the harmonious balance of the four qualities or elements</note>, and yet it unfolds itself in every life and makes itself distinct according to the property of every life.

### Note.

<margin>In the year 1624.</margin>
*Courteous Reader, this is what our author—blessed in the spirit of high knowledge—readily began to bring to an easier understanding for the simple-minded, but he has not finished it. For in the year of our Lord 1624 <note>original: "A. O. 1624" (Anno Orbis/Otii)</note>, in his Jubilee year, or the 50th year of his age, he went, according to the Mystery, back into his grave, or his magic and mental principle.* <note>This note explains that the author, Jakob Böhme, died before completing this specific explanation of his complex theology.</note>

<summary>This page explains the Holy Spirit's role as the "workmaster" of the physical and spiritual worlds, acting through the "outspoken Word" to animate all creation. It concludes with a biographical note regarding the author's death in 1624, which left the work unfinished.</summary>
