# How Man is Baptized Ch. 3.

17. Understand us now correctly here concerning the baptism of this *outward spirit*: The inward Holy Spirit of divine love kindles the *outflowing spirit* and anoints it with divine *power*. For the humanity of Christ, according to our outward spirit, was anointed by the Holy Ghost. That same outward spirit of Christ—which in Christ’s person ruled in a *human manner* within the elements of the body—now, in the covenant of his testament in water-baptism, anoints the inward ground of the *water*. This anointed power in the water, through the word of the covenant, anoints the true *man* who was created in Adam out of the *slime* <note>original: "Limus"; referring to the dust or clay of the earth from which Adam was formed</note> of the earth. So also, it anoints the *spirit of the world* <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi"</note>; namely, the right <margin>* Note.</margin> *astral soul*, which shall return on the Last Day to be *tested*.

18. Now understand us dearly and well, for this is of great value to us: This anointing of water-baptism is *anointed* with Christ’s suffering, death, and resurrection. It is anointed with Christ’s shedding of his blood, with which heavenly blood he changed God’s wrath into love and *besprinkled* death in the human property. With this blood, he anointed the earth when he *poured it out* upon and into it. Note also: the earth was anointed with it when Christ, in his death, yielded his anointed life of the outward humanity (which he received from us) back into this *spirit of the world* <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi"</note>. He anointed that spirit also with his anointing in his death: specifically, when his outward mortal soul received from us—which died on the cross and yielded itself into the *hand of God* as into his outspoken word—departed from the body into the elements. Furthermore, it is anointed because his outward soul, along with the eternal soul in which the Holy Ghost resided, penetrated through death and hell. It is also anointed because the Holy Ghost, in the Word, received this dead astral soul out of death and *brought* it through death, by his resurrection, into eternal life, where God’s love anointed it with eternal life.

19. This anointing of Christ is understood to be present in water-baptism as part of the covenant of God. For God anoints the elements of the body, together with the *mortal* and *immortal* soul, in the humanity of Christ. He brings Christ, with this anointing, into his death and into the wrath, and through death into eternal life. This anointing—by which Christ has overcome God’s wrath, sin, death, the devil, and hell—God has ordained in water-baptism through his covenant of the Old Testament <note>The author likely refers here to the continuity of God's promise from the Old to the New</note>, and he offers it now to every person.

20. And so now, if a person desires it and inclines themselves toward it as a human being and *not a beast*, then the Holy Ghost *baptizes* the inward ground—namely, his temple, which he himself possesses—which is the true eternal...

<summary>This page explains the mystical connection between Christ's physical sacrifice and the sacrament of Baptism, arguing that Christ's blood anointed the very elements of the world and the human "astral soul." It describes Baptism as a means by which the Holy Spirit applies Christ's victory over death and wrath to the individual believer.</summary>

<keywords>Baptism, Anointing, Astral Soul, Spiritus Mundi, Covenant, Christ's Blood, Resurrection, Elements, Holy Spirit</keywords>

--- Page 88 ---
eternal Spirit, together with the eternal soul. The anointing of Christ—originating from the humanity he received from us—anoints and baptizes in *us* the true first man, who was created in *Adam* out of the <term>limm</term><note>original: "Limm"; likely referring to the "limus" or slime/clay of the earth from which Adam was formed.</note> of the earth, together with the *mortal soul*. (Yet, so that a man may understand the basis of this: it is that which shall arise and live eternally.)

21. This is done through Christ’s suffering, death, and resurrection in his bloody <gloss>conflict</gloss><margin>Or, fight or combat.</margin>. Specifically, the *victory* of his death is the hand that baptizes. Note: the true man is baptized unto *life*, and the serpent-like, coarse earthly man is baptized with Christ’s *death* and dying. This is so that he shall die and leave all his evil lusts and desires to the death of Christ, so that death may kill them and bring a new will out of the death of Christ, out of both soul and body.

22. *The evil man*—that is, that serpent-monster—is seized by this baptism (specifically where the hand of God, his speaking Word itself, takes and baptizes) and is *buried* with Christ in his death and in that *hell* into which Christ entered in his dying. Understand this as the darkness of God’s wrath, namely, into the *abyss* of humanity. This anointing in the baptism binds and unites itself with the true Adamic first body, which existed before the fall of *Adam*. Through Christ’s victory, it generates a new will in soul and body which obeys God.

23. Thus, according to this anointing, the true man stands in the anointing of Christ, and the serpent-monster stands in God’s *wrath*. Yet, during this lifetime, they dwell in one body, but each in its own principle—that is, in its own property. Concerning this, *St. Paul* says in *Romans 7:20, 25*: *"Now if I sin, it is no longer I who do it, but sin that dwells in the evil flesh. So then, I serve God with the mind of the anointed ground, but with the mind of the false flesh I serve the law of sin."*

24. And he says further in *Romans 8:1, 28*: *"There is therefore now no condemnation to those who are in Christ Jesus in his anointing, who according to this purpose of God are called in the Word,"* or are thus included in the calling within the anointing. Here he means the true anointed man, who during this lifetime remains hidden in the <gloss>scales</gloss><margin>That is, the shell.</margin> of the earthly body, like fine gold in a coarse stone. Yet, according to the anointing, he *dwells in heaven*, as *St. Paul* also says in *Philippians 3:20*: *"Our citizenship is in heaven."* Also in *Romans 6:15* and *Galatians 2:17*: *"Shall we then, who belong to Christ, still be sinners? God forbid!"* In this, he means the anointed ground, and *not* the beast-like monster of earthliness, full of evil inclinations, which stands in the wrath of God and condemnation. That monster shall not inherit the kingdom of God, even though in this lifetime it still clings to the true anointed man.

<summary>This page explains the dual nature of the believer, where the "true man" is baptized into Christ's life while the "serpent-monster" of the earthly flesh is consigned to Christ's death. It uses Pauline theology to argue that while a Christian still inhabits a sinful body, their true spiritual essence—the "anointed ground"—already dwells in heaven.</summary>
<keywords>baptism, Adam, dualism, St. Paul, original sin, sanctification, Christ's victory, the abyss, anointed ground</keywords>

--- Page 89 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the spiritual nature of the Christian and begins Chapter 4, which focuses on the transition from the Old Testament covenant to the New Testament sacrament of water baptism.</meta>

25. The anointed ground <note>original: "Ground"; referring to the spiritual foundation or inner soul</note> is the Christian, and not the outward, coarse beast <note>the physical, animalistic body</note>. It is the *anointing* which daily breaks the head of the monster, the serpent—namely, the evil earthly will. This is where the struggle in man exists, where one property wills evil and the other wills good, and whichever overcomes drives the man to act. *Therefore, man's works shall and must be judged, and every one shall be gathered into its proper barn: namely, that which is done in the power of the anointing shall be gathered into the Kingdom of God; and that which is done in the power of the monster, the serpent, shall be gathered into damnation, into the darkness of wrath and death.*

---

### *The Fourth Chapter.*

## Concerning outward water baptism performed by human hands: who is worthy to perform such baptism? And what baptized infant worthily receives this baptism? How it fares with the unworthy hand, as well as with the unworthy baptized infant.

->*In these times, this is highly necessary to be considered.*<-

1. When God established the testament of his holy covenant of grace in Paradise, moving beyond the types and sacrifices, and intended to transform circumcision into water baptism, he then brought forth a *branch* out of his covenant in a human manifestation and began the water baptism through him. This is clearly seen in *John the Baptist*, who was proclaimed by an angelic message or embassy and was, moreover, from the lineage of the high priests.

2. His incarnation, or becoming man, did not come from the will of the flesh; for his father *Zachary* and mother *Elizabeth* were elderly people, advanced in years, whose physical capacity for reproduction <note>original: "fleshly sperme or seede"</note> was clearly dead, and the natural ability of man was entirely gone. The seed of *Zachary* and *Elizabeth* was raised up through the moving of the Holy Ghost out of the paradisiacal covenant of grace, within their property of reproduction <note>original: "Spermatick Property"</note> which had by then completely vanished.

<summary>This page explains that a Christian's works are judged based on whether they stem from spiritual anointing or earthly will, and introduces the divine origins of John the Baptist as the transition from circumcision to water baptism.</summary>
<keywords>anointing, spiritual warfare, John the Baptist, Covenant of Grace, Zachary and Elizabeth, water baptism, incarnation</keywords>

--- Page 90 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the spiritual lineage and office of John the Baptist in relation to the incarnation of Christ.</meta>

3. Indeed, he <note>John the Baptist</note> came out of the human ground, but not out of human *ability*. For the anointing in the Covenant awakened it, so that their sperm <note>original: "sperme"; here meaning the biological seed or essence of life</note> was unlocked above the course and power of Nature’s own ability. This *Holy Spirit* raised up a seed out of the human essence, and for that purpose chose <margin>a The Seed.</margin> it in its very beginning.

4. But the anointing of *John* was performed during *Mary’s* greeting, when she came to old *Elizabeth* and the divine anointing stirred in her (understand this to be in *Mary*) arising from *Christ’s* Incarnation, or his becoming Man. When *Mary*, full of this anointing, greeted *Elizabeth*, then the Incarnation of *Christ*—diffused with the anointing through *Mary’s* greeting—entered into *John* in his mother’s womb. This happened not only in *John*, but also in his mother *Elizabeth*, so that she was full of this anointing; and both mothers prophesied in the Spirit of this anointing.

5. And this is the Baptism of *John*: when he was anointed with the Incarnation of *Christ*, by *Christ* himself, and received the Baptism of the Holy Spirit from the Covenant while in his mother’s womb. This was so that he should be a forerunner and proclaimer, declaring that *the time of the Anointing and the fulfilling of God’s Covenant was at hand*. It signaled that God has introduced his Covenant of the anointing of *Grace* into humanity, and that the Covenant has become a Man. From this point forward, God will pour out his anointing through the humanity of *Christ* by an elementary *medium* or means. This no longer consists of *fire*, as in the Old Testament, but in the love and meekness of *water*, showing that the time of Grace is at hand, wherein God has placed his anointing into humanity.

6. Furthermore, we see that God began no new Covenant there, but only a new *ordinance*. For *John* allowed himself to be circumcised, as did *Christ*. *John* took the power and calling out of the Covenant of circumcision and out of the sacrifices of fire, and by God’s command brought them into the *Water-Baptism*. This was to signify that from then on, sin should be drowned by the death of *Christ* in his love and meekness, and *no longer burn in the fire*, as was done by *Israel* in the symbolic types <note>original: "Type"; a theological term for a person or event in the Old Testament that foreshadows something in the New Testament</note>.

7. Moreover, we see by *John* that he did not yet baptize for the forgiveness of sins, but for repentance—namely, with the Spirit of the anointing toward repentance. This sounds through the hearts of men and *prepares the way for the Spirit of Christ*, which unlocks the doors of men’s hearts and souls. This was so that after <margin>b Namely, John the Baptist.</margin> him, the *King of Glory might enter in at those opened gates*. Concerning this, *David* says: *Lift up your heads, O you gates; and be lifted up, you everlasting doors; and the King of Glory shall come in* (Psalm 24:7, 9). And as *John* witnesses: *He baptized with water unto repentance and forgiveness of sins, yet he is not Christ; but after him comes he who has the winnowing fan in his hand, who will baptize with the fire of the Spirit* (Luke 3:16, 17).

<summary>This page explains that John the Baptist received his divine anointing in the womb through Mary's visitation to Elizabeth, marking the transition from the Old Testament's "fire" sacrifices to the New Testament's "water" baptism of grace. It emphasizes John's role as the forerunner who prepares human hearts for the indwelling of Christ.</summary>

<keywords>John the Baptist, Mary, Elizabeth, Incarnation, Baptism, Covenant, Grace, Repentance, Holy Spirit, Psalm 24</keywords>

--- Page 91 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the spiritual significance of baptism, specifically focusing on the transition from the Old Testament Law to the New Testament Covenant of Grace as embodied by John the Baptist and Christ.</meta>

8. We also see in the example of *John* [the Baptist] that he had to be the son of a priest—those who were familiar with circumcision and sacrifices. He had to emerge from the Law of the Covenant and submit himself to be circumcised, taking on the Covenant, so that he might *begin* the Water-Baptism with the Spirit of the Covenant and of circumcision. This baptism is the ordinance of grace and forgiveness; for sin was no longer to be destroyed by fire, but by Love, which God manifested in the Covenant through *Christ*. Sin was to be drowned and transformed by God’s meekness.

9. Therefore, God ordained this means of Water-Baptism for the destruction of sins to signify that sin should be drowned in the blood of Christ—in his love and grace. From this point forward, humanity would have an open gate of grace to God through love and meekness, so that now, through repentant sorrow for sins and the <margin>c. The absolution from sins.</margin> $^c$ forsaking of sins, one may enter through this gate into grace.

10. And we see here in *John*, the beginner of Water-Baptism, what kind of person is worthy to baptize with this new Covenant of Grace: specifically, one who has already been *baptized* with this grace beforehand. John was such a person, having been baptized by Christ even in his mother’s womb. Flesh and blood *without* this anointing cannot baptize, for this <margin>d. Or, Power.</margin> $^d$ authority does not reside in the power of man, but in Christ’s $^d$ authority.

<margin>Note.</margin> 11. A *Christian* in whom the anointing of Christ dwells baptizes with that same anointing. For the Holy Trinity baptizes through the Grace-Covenant of the anointing—through Christ’s incarnation (becoming human), his suffering, death, and victory. Now, if anyone wishes to baptize, they must not only baptize with the hand and with water, but also with the *faith* of the anointing.

12. An unfaithful or unbelieving baptizer does no more in this high work of baptism than the stone font [or basin] does which holds the water. For though he sprinkles or pours water and uses the words of Christ, he effects nothing himself; he is silent [or dead] in the spiritual operation unless the Covenant of Christ works and baptizes. He is merely a *medium* or instrument, <margin>e. Or, Thing.</margin> like an inanimate $^e$ substance that does not itself cooperate in this Covenant; he only performs the outward act, in which God works according to His Covenant.

13. This is not to be understood as if the work performed by an unworthy <margin>f. Or, Ineffectual.</margin> $^f$ hand were therefore entirely powerless. No, the Covenant of God <margin>g. Absenteth or withdraweth.</margin> $^g$ does not separate itself simply because of an unworthy hand, which is only a medium or means. For faithful parents, who exist within the anointing of this Covenant and have "put on" the Covenant, also possess that anointing within their seed; they produce children out of the property...

<summary>This page explains that Water-Baptism represents the drowning of sin through Christ's love rather than its destruction by fire. It emphasizes that while the baptizer acts as a medium, the true power of the sacrament comes from Christ's authority and the Holy Trinity.</summary>

# Chapter 4. By Man's Hand.

property of their bodies and souls. Now, if they are baptized in body and soul and have put on the anointing of Christ, why then have they not also the *Ens* <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term referring to the essential being or spiritual substance of a thing</note> of their bodies? If they are the *Temples of the Holy Spirit* (*who dwells in them*), and eat Christ’s flesh and drink his blood—so that Christ is in them and they are in Christ, as Christ says in *John 6:56*—why then is the fruit of their bodies not also holy? For Christ says indeed (*Matthew 7:18*), *“A good tree cannot bring forth bad fruit, and a bad tree cannot bring forth good fruit.”* If the eye of the spirit is light, then the whole body is light. If the leaven is holy, then the whole lump is holy (*Matthew 6; Romans 11*).

14. John <note>John the Baptist</note> was baptized in his mother's womb through the Spirit of Christ by the sound of Mary’s voice, as was his mother Elizabeth. We see clearly how it came to pass: as Elizabeth heard the Spirit of Mary, the Holy Spirit moved in her and also in her fruit <note>her unborn child</note> alike. They received the baptism of Christ from his humanity together, the mother with the son. Why then should it not also be so now among holy parents in whom the anointing dwells? *For as the tree is, so also is its fruit.* But the Covenant in baptism exists so that everyone should, by their own will—as a particular branch on the tree and as a life of their own—put on the Covenant of Christ through the outward medium or means ordained for that purpose.

15. This is not to be understood as saying that a child of honest, anointed parents who has attained life is *not* in the anointing of Christ if it should die *before* baptism. It has indeed put on the anointing of its parents, for it has come into existence out of their substance—out of the substance of their baptized souls and bodies. If it lives, it only needs to enter into the image of the *visible* Covenant as a life of its own, and with its own will give itself up to that which it inherits from its parents. But since a child does not understand this, the parents do it with their *faith*, as does everyone called to that work—namely, the <margin>Godparents or sponsors.</margin> witnesses or godfathers and godmothers. In their faith, they desire through their prayer to <margin>Transfer or deliver over.</margin> recommend the child to the Covenant of Christ, presenting it within the Covenant of Christ to the Holy Trinity and desiring the Covenant to be upon this child.

16. All of these—the parents and those standing by, namely the faithful baptizer and the witnesses—*work* with their faith upon the nature of the child. They reach out to the child with their faith and the Covenant of Christ. For the will of their faith takes the child’s will (which is currently without understanding) into their own faith’s desire. Thus, they carry the child’s will within their own will, along with their anointing, into the Covenant of Christ before the *Holy Trinity*.

17. When the baptizer sprinkles or pours the water upon the child, his faith is present within the water and fastens itself in...

# Of Outward Water-Baptism Chapter 4

In the words of Christ, who says: *Matthew 28:19. Go hence into all the world, baptizing all nations, in the name of the Father, Son, and Holy Ghost:* Upon Christ’s command they baptize men. The baptizer baptizes with the hand and with faith; and the parents, together with the witnesses <note>godparents</note>, baptize with their faith’s desire, with the anointed will. They infuse it into Christ’s Covenant, and Christ infuses himself therein with his blood, death, and victory—namely, with the victory of his death—and kindles the faith-essence <note>original: "faiths-Ens"; the spiritual substance or being of faith</note> of the child with his love-fire.

18. Thus the grain of mustard seed of the love-fire sows itself into the essence of the child’s soul and body as a glimmering <margin>k Hatcher, breeder, or ember.</margin> mother <note>original: "Mother"; here used in the sense of a source, womb, or nurturing origin</note> of divine love. This mother-source afterwards, when the child gains understanding of its *own*, is further kindled through faith, repentance, and prayer, and becomes a high-shining light. And when afterwards the mouth of faith eats Christ’s *flesh* and drinks *his blood*, then this holy mother love-fire attains holy oil for the burning of its life.

19. With baptism is implanted the humanity of Christ according to the holy love-water—namely, the *water of eternal life*, in which water the holy fire may burn. And with the Supper <note>Holy Communion</note> is shared the holy tincture <note>a spiritual essence or medicinal virtue</note> in the blood and flesh of Christ—namely, the true burning love-fire, a spiritual anointing oil out of the divine fire and light, which the water of love (the Baptism-Covenant) kindles. And though a child be born of *holy parents*, and in the mother’s womb be clearly in this Covenant, yet it ought also to put on this Covenant in its own self-subsisting person, in its own life’s <margin>Note.</margin> will. This is because in the flesh, sin and uncleanness are propagated and inherited together; therefore, the child should put on this Covenant for itself. For the child ought and must, with the Covenant of Grace, break the head of the serpent in the flesh, and therefore it ought also to put on Christ with his victory. For Christ now offers himself to the child; it ought therefore in its own person to receive him with its own will, and give its own will up to him.

20. For baptism is nothing else but a marriage or contract with God <margin>l Text: upon.</margin> in Christ’s blood and death, wherein Christ binds himself to man with his victory and resurrection, and places man therein. And man in baptism gives up his Adam-like <note>original: "Adamicall"; referring to the fallen nature of Adam</note> contrary will to the *death of Christ*, and desires to die to his own will in the death of Christ, and to rise again through Christ’s resurrection out of Christ’s death—with and in Christ—to a new will, and to live and to will with Christ. For we are, through baptism with Christ’s victory, planted anew in the Word of God. For man is the outspoken, imaged Word of God, which has brought his will in *Adam* from the speaking of God into a private will and speaking, and has become treacherous...

<summary>This page explains that baptism is a spiritual marriage and covenant where Christ's "love-fire" is planted as a seed in the child's soul. It emphasizes that even children of believers must personally enter this covenant to overcome inherited sin and align their will with God's Word.</summary>

# Chapter 4. By Man's Hand.

...treacherous to the *Word* of God: and with the Baptism, the imaged word <note>original: "Imaged Word"; referring to the human soul as an image of God's word</note> promises and incorporates itself again with the Eternal Speaking of God, that it will speak and will according to God: namely, *God's righteousness and truth.*

<margin>In or into.</margin>
21. Baptism is an *oath* which man swears into God, when he renounces the Devil and his kingdom, and gives himself up to God as his own property, namely, to be a *Temple of God*. And though a child does not understand *this*, yet the baptizer, as well as the parents and witnesses, should understand it and introduce their faith into the child's will. Thus, with their introduced *will* joined to the child's will, they together plunge or sink themselves down with this oath into God's Covenant through the *Word* of Christ, by his command, in the water-baptism. They should take hold of the command of Christ within themselves—that is, in their faith—and so, through their *understanding*, enter into the Covenant along with the child's <margin>Or, ignorance.</margin> lack of understanding. In the place of, and with the will of the child, they make such a promise to God in their own will. The *parents* especially may and ought to do this, seeing the child has sprung out of their life and substance, as a branch grows out <margin>Thus,</margin> of a tree trunk; so they *also have power* to include the *will* of the child within the will of their faith, and with the child's will to give it up and bind or unite it unto God.

22. Moreover, the *baptizer* has this in his power, who by the command of Christ stands there in Christ's place. He brings the command of Christ in his mouth and baptizes *with his hand* upon the command of Christ. Such a person should be the *anointed* of Christ and enter into that work *through the door of Christ* <note>original: "through the Doore of Christ"; meaning entering the ministry through true spiritual calling and faith</note>, or he is merely like a piece of wood or a clod of earth standing by as an *earthly medium*. In such a case, he himself does *not* baptize along with his own faith, but is only an outward instrument of the Covenant, like the axe with which the carpenter hews. Though indeed, he is not entirely like the axe that cuts, but like the *hand* that holds the axe: he neither cuts nor works together in the work of the *Spirit*, but is only the outward instrument and performs only an outward act. He does not reach the Covenant itself, but only the water, and brings a dead word in his life; yet the office of Christ remains in the command.

23. The *living Word* in the Covenant which baptizes there does not have its origin in the power of a wicked mouth, but out of the *command* in the Covenant. The wicked mouth brings the outward *literal word* as a minister of it, but the Covenant brings the *Living Word in power*. If the parents and witnesses are faithful, they reach the child forth with their faith to the Covenant, and the wicked baptizer with his mouth and hand is but an instrument to it—in the same way that a clock strikes and sounds and yet has no life, but gives notice and understanding to the *living*...

<summary>This page explains that Baptism is a sacred oath where parents and witnesses exercise their faith on behalf of an infant, binding the child's will to God's Covenant. It also clarifies that the spiritual validity of the sacrament depends on Christ's command rather than the personal righteousness of the minister performing the rite.</summary>

# Of Outward Water Baptism, Chapter 4

...what it means: or as a wicked man, in a show of holiness, names and acknowledges the holy name of God before a *holy* person, where instantly the holy name of God is thereby stirred and becomes active in the *heart* of the holy man who hears it. Yet the hypocrite, in his own knowledge, *neither* understands it nor feels it. This stirring of the good *heart* does not originate from the wicked mouth and enter into the holy heart; rather, it proceeds out of the **Name of God.**

<margin>Note.</margin>
24. In this same way, the Holy Name baptizes even through a wicked baptizer. It is not the evil mouth that does the work, but the person must be an instrument who pronounces the Holy Name. The baptism works not because of his pronouncing it, but from the *Name* and the *Covenant*, through the faithful *parents* and *witnesses* introducing their faith. For the Covenant is itself a *faith* [or *believing*]—namely, God’s Word and *mouth*. The Covenant baptizes all who give themselves up to it, whether worthily or unworthily, and whether the faith of the baptizer, the parents, or the witnesses is present or not. However, there is a great difference in the result, as the Scripture says: *With the holy you are holy, and with the perverse you are perverse* <note>original: "Cum sancto sanctus eris, et cum perverso perversus," Psalm 18:26</note>. Also, *as the people are, so is the God they have*; and as St. Paul says of the Lord's Supper: *Therefore they received it unto judgment, because they do not discern the Lord’s body* <note>1 Corinthians 11:29</note>.

25. For where God’s *love* is present, his *wrath* is also present; where light is, there is also fire. The *essence* <note>original: "Ens"; the spiritual substance or being</note> of faith presses only through the fire out into the light. The Covenant stands firm; the child is baptized with the Covenant, and the Covenant receives the child, even if there were only a wicked baptizer and witnesses without faith. But as the mouth is, such is the food in the mouth, as the Lord says through the prophet: *Why does the wicked take my Covenant into his mouth, while he hates* <margin>Nurture or chastisement.</margin> *to be reformed?* <note>Psalm 50:16–17</note>. Men should not misuse the name of God, for the Lord will not leave him unpunished who misuses it <note>Deuteronomy 5:11</note>. He will not cast pearls before swine <note>Matthew 7:6</note>. The wicked man who draws near to His Covenant is to God a "good savor" <note>a pleasing aroma</note> leading to the condemnation of death, while to the holy, he is a good savor leading to life <note>2 Corinthians 2:15–16</note>.

26. What else can happen if there are evil parents without faith, but that they also beget wicked children? For as the tree is, so is the fruit. And if they do not convert, and through true, earnest repentance and prayer incorporate their fruit <note>their children</note> into Christ, and send them with faith to this Covenant—but instead invite wicked witnesses or godparents <note>original: "Gossips"; an archaic term for godparents or sponsors at a baptism</note> who lack faith, and there is also a wicked baptizer—who shall baptize here? Shall the love of God in the Covenant of Grace baptize? Might it not be as St. Paul says of the Supper: *That the wicked receive it unto judgment?* Now, if a man makes a mockery of the Covenant, how will God’s glory appear among the scorners?

# Chapter 4. By Man's Hand.

Scorners? There it is rightly said: *With the holy you are holy, and with the perverse you are perverse* <note>original: "Cum sancto sanctus eris, et cum perverso perversus," quoting Psalm 18:25-26</note>. If the seed be <margin>Or, False.</margin> bad, even if a man casts it into good ground, bad fruit still grows from it; for it draws from the good ground an *essence* <note>original: "Ens," a philosophical term for the fundamental nature or being of a thing</note> of its own likeness to itself.

27. How then can it go well there, where a child springs from parents who are entirely wicked, and where only wicked witnesses are called to the ceremony? These witnesses stand there only for status and pride, or for human favor, and are called for such reasons. In such a case, there is no faith or goodwill, much less an earnest prayer; instead, only an *earthly Lucifer* <note>A reference to prideful, fallen humanity acting in the spirit of the devil</note> stands before the Covenant of Christ, and the one performing the baptism is also a dead person <note>Spiritually dead or lacking living faith</note>. Who awakens the Holy Covenant here? As the faith is that moves or stirs the Covenant, such is also the manifestation of the Covenant, and so is the baptism.

28. Christ says: *Suffer little children to come unto me, for of such is the Kingdom of God* (Matthew 10:14, Chapter 19:13). But he means children, not wolves and beasts. He bids them to come to him, and *not* to bring them to him in the devil's pride and status, with stately, high-minded, false, and unfaithful people who do nothing but despise the humility of Christ and scorn love. It must be done in earnest if a man will enter through the wrath of God into love.

29. Reason says this: "What does it matter to the child that it is begotten of wicked parents and is brought by evil people to the Covenant of Christ?"

Answer: Yes indeed, what is it to God—who does not will the death of a sinner—that wicked parents beget wicked children, and that a man does nothing but scorn his Covenant of Grace? Shall he therefore cast the pearl before swine? The parents indeed know very well that God says: *He will punish the sin of the parents on the children unto the third and fourth generation* (Deuteronomy 5:9). Shall he then pour his love into their wicked wills, when they do not actually desire that love? There is no earnestness there, but merely the performance of a custom; they do it only with a hypocritical show without sincerity, and furthermore, they use people for the ceremony who only scorn the simplicity of Christ with their pride.

30. To be familiar with the Testaments <note>The sacraments or divine promises</note> and Covenant of Christ, and to make use of them, will require great earnestness—not only in *outward show* but in *spiritual power*. For the Covenant of God in Christ is made through his blood and death for this purpose: that whoever will partake of this Covenant and these Testaments should *convert with their will* and enter into repentance. They must die to their inherited false lusts within the Covenant through Christ’s death, and be *newborn* out of the Covenant of these Testaments.

31. For baptism consists not of water alone, but in the...

# Of the Outward Water-Baptism Chapter 4

31. The Word of God and Faith: The water is but a medium wherein the Word of God and Faith take hold and work. Without the Word of God and Faith, there is no baptism. The faith of man must take hold of the promised Word, and in the water <margin>Or, Essence.</margin> these two—namely, Faith and the Word of God—bring themselves into one <insert>substance</insert>, and that spiritual substance is the baptism which is understood to be within the water. Now, if there is no faith at this work, then the Word is without human substance, existing only in itself according to and within the Covenant; that baptizes a person according to the person's own nature <note>original: "Property"</note>. But faith in Grace apprehends the Grace in the Covenant and Word, and brings itself with the Covenant into the water; and so then the Word of God, the Faith, and the Water all baptize together.

32. For the outward, elementary water is not the ground of the baptism, but rather the spiritual water, which is united and tied with the Word in the Covenant and with Faith. For the *Word* pours <margin>Text: One.</margin> itself forth in a water-source of life in great meekness and love, and *Faith* takes hold of this promised Word of Grace in the Covenant. This Grace in the spiritual water joins itself with the elementary water, in the same way that the invisible world made itself visible through the substance of this world—namely, with the elements. And as the *invisible* Word of God works through the *visible*, and as the visible substance of the elements shall eventually return to the invisible, so also in the Baptism-Covenant: the invisible element (the heavenly substance) unites itself with the visible elements of *man's* body. This is the substance of eternity joining with the substance of time; the eternally speaking Word of Divine Love joining with <margin>Or, Formed.</margin> the outspoken, <insert>imaged</insert> word of humanity.

33. For the Pure Element—belonging to the holy spiritual world from which the four physical elements sprang—is that which baptizes *through* the four elements of man's body. The true man created in *Adam*, who is stuck inside the gross husk of the four elements, is baptized unto eternal life. For the Pure Element soaks or *infuses* itself again into the vanished image of man—an image that originated from the Pure Element but faded in *Adam*. And to <margin>Or, Instituted.</margin> that end, God has <insert>ordained</insert> his Covenant in the water-baptism. Now, *Faith* and *Repentance* are required for this receiving. Repentance is an unlocking or stirring of the hidden, shut-up parts of man, whereby the inward spiritual desire—the spiritual mouth for such receiving—is opened. Faith is the opened mouth which takes in the Holy Element.

34. And although a child *without* understanding cannot do this, yet those who baptize and the others present at the baptism should do it with their faith; for their faith must encompass itself

<summary>The author explains that baptism is a spiritual union where the Word of God and human faith meet within the medium of water to restore the "Pure Element" to humanity's fallen nature. He emphasizes that while the water is physical, the transformation is an internal, spiritual infusion that requires an "opened mouth" of faith.</summary>

<keywords>Baptism, Covenant, Faith, Word of God, Pure Element, Humanity, Repentance, Adam, spiritual substance, sacraments</keywords>

--- Page 98 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of infant baptism and the spiritual role of the community and parents in the sacrament.</meta>

in the child's will, for the child has *not yet* a will, neither for Good nor Evil. Instead, their Faith <note>The faith of the parents and witnesses.</note> encompasses itself within the child's Life—namely, in Soul and Spirit. This is possible because all souls have their origin from One, and so they are in the Center, on one single Ground: the Word of God. Out of this Word the Soul has taken its origin, and within it all souls stand together in the Ground. For this "encompassing" in the child is nothing else but to introduce their *love-will* into the child. Although a man cannot do that by his *own power* and might, yet if the will of man anchors itself in *God's Word* and Promise, and takes hold of the *Promised Grace*—together with the command that we should perform this act—then the *possibility* is there. For the Promised Word gives and works the ability in man's will, and performs the deed: God's will takes man's will, which has been surrendered to Him, and *does it* through His might *using man's will*.

35. Therefore I say, it is a dangerous thing to baptize children without the Faith of the parents, of those who baptize, and of the rest who are present for the work. The Scripture says, *Hebrews 11:6: Without Faith it is impossible to please God.* But this Faith is not just a history or knowledge that a man merely *assents* to, believing it is God's work. No, it is an earnest, desiring *co-working*; a pressing into the Promised Grace; an earnest consideration of our inherited sin and of the great seriousness of God; and how He, through this *Grace-Covenant*, receives us again as children and pours His Grace into us *through this work*.

<margin>Turning into God.</margin>
36. Those who are present should, with great humility and <term>inversion</term> <note>original: "inversion"; meaning an internal turning of the soul toward God.</note> to God, with earnest prayer, apply themselves to this Covenant. They should well consider what they intend to do there, for they stand even before the Covenant of the Holy Trinity, before God's open Face, and are dealing with both God and man. They should well *imprint upon themselves* the bitter sufferings and death of Jesus Christ, into which a child is baptized, and of which they are *co-working* witnesses according to the Faith. They should not stand before this Covenant like a *prostitute before a mirror* with proud hearts, as if it were a worldly honor to be exploited. Such people, who have no understanding or faith toward the act and only come to it with proud hearts, are not <margin>Or, furtherance.</margin> helpful to this Covenant, but a *hindrance*. They hinder others, who gape at the pride and finery and thereby forget the seriousness of the occasion.

37. And although the Baptism *may* be performed without such persons, through the earnestness and prayer of the parents and the baptizer, yet the Christian Church has ordained that living witnesses should be present to attend to this serious work with their prayers. This was well ordained, but it has come to such a misuse that

<summary>The author argues that infant baptism requires the active, earnest faith of parents and witnesses to be effective, as they act as spiritual conduits for God's grace. He warns against treating the sacrament as a vain social display rather than a solemn spiritual covenant.</summary>
<keywords>infant baptism, Grace-Covenant, co-working faith, spiritual origin, humility, witnesses, Holy Trinity</keywords>

--- Page 99 ---
It would be much better if the children of infidelity <note>original: "Infidelity"; referring here to those without true faith</note> were not present, for the Devil has no place in this work. Now, if the Devil has the chief dominion in a man, of what use is such a man at this ceremony? As mentioned before, the baptismal font and the unfaithful bystander are equally ineffective, except that the proud man is a hindrance to others.

<margin>Or, significance.</margin>
38. Baptism is a substantial working <note>meaning an actual, spiritual transformation</note>, not a mere sign or symbol of the Testament of Christ. The Holy Spirit baptizes the soul and the spirit with Christ’s blood, death, and victory. Furthermore, the holy element of the spiritual water baptizes the body (made of the four elements) for the resurrection of the dead; it baptizes the serpent’s essence <note>original: "Ens"; the seed of sin or original corruption</note> and the awakened, introduced poison so that it may die.

39. It is a substantial infusion of the Covenant of God. Therefore, if the infant being baptized is to worthily receive it, there must be earnestness. A Christian—one who is truly a Christian in Christ, in whom Christ works, lives, and dwells—should be the one to baptize. *For he must enter into this sheepfold by the door of Christ—namely, through Christ’s Spirit—and not climb up some other way; otherwise, he is a thief and a murderer who comes only to rob and steal the honor of Christ and deceive men. He should be a true shepherd and not a hireling.* <note>A reference to John 10:1-13</note> Here, mere babbling and putting on a show are of no value; there must be earnestness, for it is a serious matter with God. All quarreling <note>original: "jangling"</note> and disputation about this work is unprofitable and even harmful. Men should take hold of it with sincerity, follow the command of Christ, and believe what Christ has said.

<margin>Note: The text says "is."</margin>
<margin>Note.</margin>
40. Nothing more belongs to this work but faith, water, and earnest prayer in true repentance, with a will that one person would help release another from death, the Devil, and Hell, and help to introduce them into the Kingdom of God. That is the whole process required here. *Everyone who is a true Christian in Christ is worthy to be a bystander and present at this work. But he who is not such a person is unworthy, whether he be the one baptizing or a bystander; one is the same as the other, for there is no respect of persons with God. In Christ we are one; He is the trunk and we are the branches. He performs His work through His fellow members, just as the trunk of a tree produces its fruit through its twigs and branches. The trunk uses no foreign tree for its twigs; likewise, Christ only uses His own members for the work of His fruit.*

<margin>Note.</margin>

Wherein *Babel* <note>original: "Babell"; a common seventeenth-century metaphor for the confused and corrupt state of the established Church</note>, the great city upon Earth,

# An Intimation or Instruction

->to the READER.<-

Though indeed I here dive and search sublimely and deep, and set it down very clearly, yet the reader must be told this much: that without the Spirit of God, it will be a mystery to him, and not understood.

Therefore, let everyone look well to how he judges, so that he does not fall into the judgment of God and be captivated by his own *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; in Behmenist thought, this refers to a state of spiritual disturbance, wrath, or the "awakened" source of destruction within the soul</note>, and so that his own reason does not distract him. I say this with good intentions, and give it to the reader to ponder in his mind.

->JACOB BEHM <note>Jacob Boehme (1575–1624), the German Christian mystic and theologian</note> of old Seidenberg.<-

<summary>Jacob Boehme warns the reader that his profound spiritual insights can only be understood through the Spirit of God rather than human reason alone. He cautions against judgmental attitudes that might lead to spiritual confusion or "Turba."</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, mysticism, Spirit of God, Turba, divine judgment, Seidenberg, spiritual discernment</keywords>

# The Contents of the Chapters in this Second little Book, Concerning the Holy Supper of Jesus Christ.

1. Concerning the foundation of the Old Testament: how this Testament was clearly represented as a *Type* <note>original: "Type"; a symbolic foreshadowing or prophetic model</note> among the *Jews*.

2. Concerning the Supper of the New Testament: how the Type has transitioned into *Essence* [or Substance].

3. How the *Disciples* ate and drank the flesh and blood of Christ.

4. Concerning the *difference* in the participation of it: what the wicked receive with this Testament, and how a person should prepare themselves so that they may be truly worthy.

5. Concerning the contention and *strife* in the high schools or universities about Christ’s Testaments: how that strife, which has filled the face of the Earth, is a vain and very harmful thing. It arises in the anger of God from the Devil’s desire and will, whereby all brotherly love and unity is destroyed, the highly precious Name of GOD is blasphemed, and the Holy GHOST—in his divine wonders—is judged and despised by *Reason* <note>original: "Reason"; here referring to human intellect operating without spiritual illumination</note>.

## Of the Foundation of the Old Testament; how this Testament was clearly shown in the Type among the Jews.

### I.

<margin>
A stifling or suffocating.
</margin>
When God sent *Moses* to *Pharaoh* and commanded him to let the children of *Israel* go into the wilderness and sacrifice to Him, and *Pharaoh* would not do it, then God sent great plagues and punishments upon him. Lastly, He sent the destroying angel, who slew all the *firstborn in Egypt*. There He commanded *Israel* the *Passover*, or to slay and eat the *Paschal Lamb*: specifically, *a lamb of a year old, and to sprinkle the doors and upper posts with its blood, so that the slaying anger should there* <margin>pass by</margin> *pass over and kill no one in the house* (Exodus 12). <note>original: Exod. 2. 12. The author likely intended Exodus 12.</note>

2. This was a *type* <note>A "type" is a person, event, or thing in the Old Testament believed to foreshadow something in the New Testament.</note> of the New Testament, showing how the posts and doors of our *life* should be sprinkled with the blood of the Lamb, *Christ*, so that God’s anger in soul and body may not swallow us up into His wrath. And just as they had to eat that lamb entirely and leave nothing remaining, so would the Lamb, *Christ*, give Himself in His Testament wholly as food for His Christendom, undivided. He would sprinkle the posts of our life with His blood of *love*, so that the destroying angel in God’s anger might not take hold of us and slay us.

3. We also have an image or likeness of this in the *unleavened cakes* which they were required to bake and eat. This showed that through this figure of the Paschal Lamb, which signified Christ, they should be a *new* and sweet <margin>dough</margin> <note>original: lump</note> when they ate the true Paschal Lamb, Christ, in His Testament. All of this was a figure of the *new regeneration* <note>The process of being "born again" or spiritually renewed.</note>, signifying how that rebirth would be born through the true Paschal Lamb, Christ (1 Corinthians 5:7), and how the New Birth would eat the sweet food of Divine Grace.

4. This is a powerful figure of the terrible Fall in *Adam*, and then of the New Birth or regeneration in *Christ*. For in *Adam*, the first birth—namely, the first angelic life—was slain by God’s anger; but by the Covenant of Grace in *Christ*, that same life was brought back again and spoken (or *inspired*) into *Adam* and *Eve*. Thus, the Spirit of God signifies by this figure of *Moses* in *Egypt* how that same "inspoken" grace should be fulfilled with heavenly and divine *substance*. It also shows how man should be <margin>smeared or streaked</margin> marked and sprinkled by that mark: namely—

<summary>This page explains the Passover in Egypt as a "type" or prophetic symbol of Christ, comparing the blood of the Paschal Lamb to Christ's sacrifice and the unleavened bread to spiritual regeneration.</summary>
<keywords>Passover, Paschal Lamb, Moses, Pharaoh, Type and Antitype, Blood of Christ, Regeneration, New Birth, Adam and Eve, Covenant of Grace</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how humanity is restored to its original angelic image through the sacrifice of Christ, using the Old Testament sacrificial laws as a symbolic blueprint.</meta>

by the blood of the Lamb of God: and how God would give him the substance of his sweet Love for food, whereby the natural, soul-like fire-life should be again quickened, and attain a Divine *Ens* <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term meaning "essence" or "being"</note> in its life-essence, and thereby be transmuted and changed again into the Angelic image.

5. Also we see this image very clearly in the sacrifices of *Israel*: how they had to slay and sacrifice beasts and burn the fat. For when God gave the Law to *Moses*, so that *Israel* should live in full obedience in the covenant of this Law, then he gave him also the figure, showing <margin>Released or reconciled. *Leviticus 8:14–21*</margin> how this Law should be fulfilled, and how man should be <insert>reconciled</insert> from sin and the anger of God. We may clearly see this in the *Third Book of Moses* <note>The Book of Leviticus</note> in the eighth chapter, where the image of the reconciliation or atonement stands.

->Where it is written thus:<-

<margin>Sacrifice.</margin> *And Moses caused a heifer to be brought for a sin-offering, and Aaron with his sons laid their hands upon its head. There it was slain, and Moses took the blood, and with his finger put it round about upon the horns <margin>Cleansed it from sin.</margin> of the altar, and cleansed the altar, and poured out the blood at the foot of the altar, and sanctified it, so that he made an atonement on it. And he took all the fat upon the entrails, and the fatty membrane upon the liver, and the two kidneys with the fat on them, and burned it upon the altar. But the heifer with its hide, flesh, and dung, he burnt with fire outside the camp, as the Lord had commanded him. And he brought a ram for a burnt sacrifice; and Aaron with his sons laid their hands upon its head, and it was slain there. And Moses sprinkled the blood round about upon the altar, and cut the ram in pieces, and burnt the head, the pieces, and the rump. And he washed the entrails and the legs with water, and so burnt the whole ram upon the altar; this was a burnt sacrifice for a sweet savor, an offering by fire to the Lord as the Lord had commanded him. (Leviticus 8:14–21)*

6. This is a true figure <note>A symbolic representation or "type"</note> of the sacrifice of Christ with our assumed humanity: how he has offered our humanity, through the sacrifice of his body, to the anger of God; and how God, in this sacrifice, has smelled his sweet love in the humanity of Christ, and reconciled his wrath in the fire.

### 7. The Figure of Christ stands thus:

<margin>*Leviticus 8:10–12*</margin> *When Moses was to make this sacrifice, he took first the anointing oil, and anointed the tabernacle <note>original: "dwelling place"</note> and all that was therein, and sanctified it, and sprinkled with it seven times upon the altar, and anointed the altar and all its implements, the washbasin with its stand, so that it was sanctified.*

# Chapter 1. Showing how this Testament was in the Type.

And he poured the anointing oil upon Aaron's head and anointed him, so that he was sanctified.

7. **Moses** stands here as a figure of God, and **Aaron** stands as a figure of Christ according to our humanity. The **Heifer** stands as a figure of the earthly, perished Adam, according to his beast-like nature; and the **Ram** stands as a figure of the true man created in Adam, showing how that same nature should be offered up to God again in the humanity of Christ. **Moses** anointed **Aaron**; that is, God anointed our humanity in Christ with His highest love—namely, with the *substantial Wisdom* and Divine substance in the name JESUS.

8. And this is the meaning: that **Moses** first anointed the Tabernacle, which signifies the body of Mary in which GOD became MAN. Therefore, the Angel called her *the blessed among all women* (Luke 1:28). For God first sanctified the Tabernacle and sprinkled His anointing oil of love upon all the seven properties of the natural life. This life in the humanity of Christ was signified by the *Altar of Moses*, which God anointed when JESUS assumed man's life. The *Laver with its foot* <note>The "laver" was a large basin used by priests for ritual washing.</note> signifies how God has anointed the human hand or *desire* in the humanity of Christ, with which He would perform wonders. The anointed *Head of Aaron* signifies how the humanity of Christ—namely, our human *science* <note>original: "Science"; here meaning knowledge, consciousness, or the rational mind.</note>—should be anointed with the Holy Ghost.

9. God set this forth through **Moses** in the *Type* <note>A "type" is a person, event, or ritual in the Old Testament believed to foreshadow something in the New Testament.</note>. For when **Moses** had anointed **Aaron**, along with the Tabernacle and the sons of **Aaron**, and established the whole business, he then caused a heifer to be brought for a sin-offering. This **Heifer** signified the *gross earthly man* shaped through **Adam's** lust. On this heifer, **Aaron** and his sons had to lay their hands upon its head. This signifies how God in Christ—and then the priests or *Pharisees*—would lay their hands upon our *mortal humanity* assumed by Christ. It signifies how they would flay (that is, put to death) our humanity according to the substance of this world, and how His human blood should be sprinkled all around the horns of the holy altar with the finger of God—namely, as if by the laying hold of God's anger. It shows how God would thus cleanse His altar in humanity, upon which altar (the *human life*) holy sacrifices should be offered to God again.

10. But the fact that **Moses** burned *the fat of the liver and kidneys* upon the altar signifies that our true Adamic *man*, made from the *slime* <note>original: "Limus"; Latin for mud or clay, referring to the dust of the ground from which Adam was formed.</note> of the earth, should not be cast away or thrust out from God according to its true inward *ground* <note>The "ground" refers to the fundamental essence or soul of a person.</note>. Instead, it should be kindled in the fire of God's anger with the fire of love and be offered to God. This was then accomplished in the humanity of Christ when He sacrificed our humanity to the anger of God, yet introduced it into God's presence with His love through...

through Death into *Life*, just as a clear Light springs up out of the fire, or as a new Life—consisting of power and Love—emerges from the dying fire.

11. But the fact that *Moses burned the heifer with its hide, flesh, and dung with fire outside the camp*, and only sprinkled the blood of the heifer upon the horns of the Altar and ignited the fat, signifies that the gross, bestial body of our flesh (with skin and bones) shall not come upon God’s Altar or inherit the kingdom of God. Instead, it shall be consumed by the essential fire of the Earth *without the holy Fire of God*. Just as *Moses* had to burn the heifer outside the camp, so also must the gross, bestial man be burned up outside the City of God—namely, in its own principle, by that same fire of nature, as we see happening before our eyes. But this earthly man’s blood, in which the astral soul <note>The "astral soul" refers to the spirit derived from the stars and planets that governs the physical body and emotions.</note> lives, should be sprinkled upon the horns of the Altar. This signifies the true man created in *Adam* out of the *dust* <note>original: "Limus"; the slime or clay of the earth from which Adam was formed.</note> of the earth with the true *star* <note>original: "Astrum"; the celestial influence or spiritual essence.</note>, whose blood or power shall return at the *Last Day*. That same blood was in Christ’s humanity (including the heavenly blood) and was sprinkled upon the <margin>i. Note.</margin> horns of the Altar during his suffering and dying on the <sup>i</sup> tree of the Cross. This was a sign that our blood, in a true human manner, has come upon God’s Altar with Christ, and that God in Christ has marked us therewith on the *Eternal Heavenly Altar*.

12. But the fact that the rest of the blood had to be poured at the foot of the Altar signifies that our human blood—in which our *outward* human life consists and which *dies* here—is poured out at the foot of the Altar (that is, into the four elements) at the moment of death. It is reserved there until the restoration of the first true man, so that the true life shall be kept at the foot of the Altar—that is, in its own principle, as in the *Great Mystery in the Spirit of the World* <note>original: "Myſterio Magno in Spiritu Mundi"; the hidden spiritual foundation of the physical universe.</note>. It remains there until God shall purge this Altar of the four elements and bring forth again the same pure virtue and power of the blood, together with the elements, and bring them into the *balanced state* <note>original: "Temperature"; a state of perfect harmony where no element dominates another.</note>. Then, the human blood shall return to the soul according to the property of the Spiritual World.

13. For this reason, *Moses* and *Aaron* had to pour out the heifer’s blood at the foot of the Altar in this symbolic ritual. God was not concerned with the blood of beasts, but he was establishing the *figure* of man’s restoration. He signifies by this that we men, according to the outward, gross, earthly man, were merely such beasts, and this gross, bestial property had swallowed up the true man into itself. Thus, he signifies how he would use his power to bring back and newly generate that same devoured internal ground. And just as the power and virtue of our *mummy* <note>original: "Mumia"; the vital essence or spiritual balsam contained within the physical body.</note>—the oily property—shall be ignited again through the fire of God (just as *Moses* ignited the fat), so shall the pow—

<summary>This page explains the symbolic meaning of Mosaic animal sacrifices, arguing that the burning of the heifer represents the destruction of man's "bestial" nature while the blood represents the preservation of the true human essence for resurrection.</summary>

# Chapter 1. Showing how this Testament was in the Type.

...of the inward ground <note>original: "Inward Ground"; the deep, essential spiritual core of a person</note>, here even in the time of *this life*, be kindled with the Divine fire, and burn upon God’s Altar. This should be done *in us* through the *unction* <note>original: "Unction"; anointing or the presence of the Holy Spirit</note> or anointing of the Spirit of God. Just as *Moses* kindled the fat, so should the spirit of Christ also kindle our inward ground even in this lifetime. And when this comes to be done, then should the heifer—namely, the "beast-man" <note>the carnal or animalistic nature of humanity</note>—be slain. That is, he should be daily *mortified* <note>original: "Mortified"; put to death or subdued through spiritual discipline</note>, and with his will and beastly desires be cast out from the host of God; for he is but earth and a beast, and *not fit for the Kingdom of Heaven* (John 6).

14. But the ram now signifies the true anointed man in the humanity of Christ, as well as our inward ground, which becomes living again through Christ’s anointing in us. Just as God, through *Aaron’s* sons—namely, through the high priest—would lay the hand of His anger upon *it* and *slay it* so that our human will might be slain, so shall the blood of this ram—namely, the *true humanity*—be sprinkled everywhere upon the Altar of God in this slaying. The human will shall be split in pieces and quite forsake its own will, and the *head*—namely, the human senses or *thoughts*—shall be kindled with God’s fire. Just as *Moses* kindled the head of the ram, as well as the pieces and the rump, so must our humanity in Christ also be washed with the water of love, and afterwards be sacrificed upon the Altar of God—namely, on the tree of the Cross.

<margin>Note.</margin>
15. Note: The fact that the ram must be kindled with fire and be offered to the Lord *for a sweet savor to the Lord* contains the Great Mystery. The ram is the true man created in *Adam*, which Christ (as the Word or the power of God) took upon Himself from our Adamic humanity in the body of *Mary*. The ram signifies that God in Christ would thus anoint our true Adamic humanity with the holy anointing oil and bring it back to God again.

16. But seeing that the human will had departed from God and had brought itself into earthly, beastly lust, this ram—namely, the humanity of Christ—had to be kindled with God’s fire of anger. For the anger of God had become manifest in humanity; therefore, God first anointed the humanity with the *Spirit* of His love in Christ’s humanity, and afterwards offered this ram—the true Adamic humanity—to the fire of God. This was done so that, in the anointing of the "love-oil," the anger-fire might be kindled, and through that oil of His holy substance, the anger-fire might be transmuted <note>original: "transmuted"; transformed or changed in nature</note> and wholly turned into a *burning of love*.

17. For our human soul had become an anger-fire—namely, an *enmity* against God—and therefore God reconciled it in the love of its substance. That is, God’s anger in the human life was thus reconciled, for God’s power smelled (or drew) the human life back into the Eternal Word—namely, into God—through the fire. God’s anger-fire...

<summary>The author explains the spiritual symbolism of animal sacrifice, equating the ram with Christ's humanity and the "true man" within us, which must be purified by God's love-fire to transform human anger into divine love.</summary>
<keywords>Atonement, Christology, Mortification, Adamic Humanity, Mysticism, Sacrifice, Transmutation, Holy Spirit</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how God’s love interacts with human nature, specifically focusing on the spiritual mechanics of sacrifice as a precursor to Christ.</meta>

was the *Mouth*, which received the human life back into it, and swallowed up the averted human will into itself.

18. But when this Great Love had given itself up into the human life in Christ, then God’s "anger-fire"—with its devouring nature in the soul’s fire—was turned into mere Love, namely: wholly into a "love-fire." And thus *Death* in the fire of God’s anger, which holds the soul captive with its impression (wherein the soul was a darkness, or a dark fire), was broken open and turned again into the Light-Life.

19. And that is what God sets forth as a *Type* <note>original: "Type"; a prophetic symbol or foreshadowing</note> of the coming of Christ through this sacrifice. For *Moses* says: *This was a sweet savor to the Lord* <note>Exodus 29:18</note>. Now, God does not favor the bestial <note>animal-like or carnal</note> life, for that shall not inherit the Kingdom of God. God’s business which he conducted through *Moses* was not about the ram and the heifer; rather, he set the humanity represented by them before him. God’s imagination <note>divine creative thought or focus</note> went into the humanity through his "inspoken" <note>internally spoken or infused</note> Grace-Covenant—that is, in that "inspoken" Grace-word of Love which stood as a goal or limit in Man, even until the time of *Mary*, when it manifested itself in humanity. God’s imagination entered into this, and Man’s imagination entered into this *Type*—the sacrifice in the fire. Thus, the incorporated Grace-Covenant also went with Man’s imagination into the sacrifice of the fire, as a *Type* signifying how Christ should reconcile God’s anger within humanity.

20. And thus was the human will ransomed in the fire through the Type of Christ. For the incorporated Grace-Covenant went together with the human desire—expressed through their *prayer*, which pressed into God by means of this sacrifice in the fire—into the fire of the sacrifice. There, it reconciled (that is, destroyed) the earthly, bestial property of the human will in the fire with Love. For with the fire, God formed an image before him as a substance; in this fire-substance, God’s fire imaged itself and reconciled the elementary fire <note>the physical/energetic life-force</note> in Man. His eternal love-fire *favored* the human will through the incorporated Grace, using the *medium* of the fire; for *Moses* had holy fire with which he kindled the sacrifice.

21. Thus was the elementary fire of Man reconciled in the Holy Fire. Yet a bestial *medium*—namely, the flesh of beasts—had to enter the Holy Fire of *Moses*, seeing as Man had become bestial. This was so that the bestial nature might be burned away in the Holy Fire through the Father’s anger-fire, and so that God’s love-fire might kindle the human soul-fire in its introduced desire within the sacrifice. And so, God’s desire in the Word *smelled* Man’s desire through the fire; for in the fire, the bestial vanity of Man’s will burned away in the anger-fire. Then the clear human will pressed into God’s love-fire as a *sweet savor*; for the incorporated, paradisical, "inspoken" Grace pressed into God along with the clear will of Man.

<summary>The author explains the spiritual significance of Mosaic animal sacrifices as a symbolic "Type" of Christ, where the "anger-fire" of God consumes the bestial nature of man to allow the human soul to reunite with God's "love-fire."</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the Old Testament sacrifices served as a "type" or symbolic precursor to the New Testament sacrament of Christ's body and blood.</meta>

22. And that is what *Moses* says: *It was a sacrifice of a sweet savor unto the Lord*. For God desired to smell nothing there but human will—specifically, the human life which existed in the Word of God before the world began. This life existed without a physical body <note>original: "without Creature"</note> but was present in power, which was then breathed into the created image of man. God "smelled" that same life through the sacrifice in the *essence* <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for the fundamental substance or being of a thing</note> of Christ—that is, through the spoken promise of grace. He reconciled the turned-away human will through grace in the fire, so that the human will became divine again. He brought (1) the fire of human life and (2) the fire of God’s love into one fire, as if into *one burning life*. That was a true sacrifice of reconciliation, or a sin-offering, where sin was offered to the fire of God’s anger to be consumed.

23. In this way, the animal flesh—which they offered and later ate—was also sanctified for man, because God’s imagination <note>In this context, "imagination" refers to God's creative focus or spiritual influence entering into the physical world.</note> in the Covenant entered into it. Therefore, *Moses* calls it *Holy Flesh*; it was also called *Holy Bread*, such as the *showbread* <note>The twelve loaves of bread placed in the Jewish Temple as an offering; see 1 Samuel 21:6 and Matthew 12:4.</note>. All of this stood as a "type" or symbol, signifying how that same indwelling power of the Covenant of Grace would manifest itself with heavenly substance in Christ. It would be given as food for the human soul—specifically for the "fire" of the soul—and in that food, the soul's fire would be transformed into a fire of love.

<margin>Note.</margin>
24. For in the Old Testament, this sweet Covenant of Grace gave itself as food into the sacrifice of the fiery desire of the soul, acting as the soul's mouth within the fire. The soul—that is, the mouth of the soul's faith—did eat of this sweet grace in the fire of sacrifice. They did *not* eat it in physical substance, but in power, looking forward to the fulfillment that was to come, until that power was manifested in the flesh. Meanwhile, their physical bodies ate the blessed bread and flesh, which also contained the power of grace (the spiritual influence of the Covenant). Thus, the *Jews* ate Christ’s flesh and drank his blood in the *essence* of grace—in power and in symbol—at a time when that power was not yet flesh and blood. Nevertheless, that same Word of Grace, which later became man, was present within it.

25. But when the time was fulfilled and that same indwelling Word of Grace (which the soul received through the desire of faith in the fiery essence) *became man*, it then gave itself as food to this "mouth of faith"—the fiery essence of the soul—according to both its divine and human properties. We will write more extensively about this in the following sections and establish the true foundation of how *Christ’s flesh and blood are to be eaten and drunk*. This will not be based on mere imagination or opinion, but on the foundation of Scripture and certain knowledge through God’s grace.

This establishes a theological link between ancient Jewish rituals and the Christian sacrament of the Eucharist.</summary>

# The Second Chapter.

## Of the Supper of the New Testament, how the *Type* <note>original: "Type"; a symbolic foreshadowing or prophetic model</note> is come into Substance.

-> I. <-

<margin>a Or, Immodeled.</margin>
When the time was *fulfilled* that this Covenant of Grace should manifest itself and take on Humanity with both Soul and Body, then the *Type* ceased. For the anger-fire of God in the soul and in the flesh of man was together ᵃ imprinted <note>original: "imprinted" or "immodeled"; shaped into a specific form</note> in the Substantial Word—that is, in the Divine *Being* <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for essence or being</note>. This Divine Being, out of divine power, also took on the Divine Being of Adam, which had vanished regarding the Kingdom of Heaven (namely, the Holy Working Fire) when the soul *imaged* itself into earthliness. He made it living again—that is, burning within itself—through the *Holy* Fire, where the Divine Sacrifice in the fire was then manifested in Humanity.

2. For the soul was of the Father’s property, according to the power of his fire, and had become a mere fire of *Anger*. *This the Father gave to the Son*, as the Manifested Covenant of Grace, and brought the Father’s anger-fire in the soul into the Son’s love-fire. Thus, the Father’s anger-fire and the Son’s love-fire stood upon one Ground in the human substance. The love-fire became substantial—that is, a heavenly flesh—and gave itself to the anger-fire of the Father in the property of human *life* as food for a burning fire. This happened in the same way as when a man adds a pleasant oily balsam to a fire; the fire, in its burning, then gives forth a pleasant smell and virtue from that balsam oil.

3. Thus the Father now *smelled this pleasant aroma* in the Humanity within the soul’s fire—the same aroma he formerly smelled in the symbolic sacrifice. For Christ was now the true High Priest, who offered to God his Father the acceptable sacrifice of sweet aroma in his Humanity, and reconciled the *Anger* within Humanity. The Altar of God upon which *Moses* sacrificed was now in the Humanity of Christ. He offered up to God the sweet humanity from the heavenly *Being*, along with the Adam-like humanity, into his anger-fire which burned in the Adam-like humanity. Sin in the Adam-like flesh was destroyed through the sweet heavenly flesh—namely, through the Substantial Word of Love, through JESUS, which is God’s greatest sweetness.

4. God brought the sacrifice of *Moses*—namely, the reconciliation in fire—

# Chapter 2. Showing how the Type is come into Substance.

5. And we see this eminently in the Supper of *Christ*. When he was finally ready to enter into his sufferings and kill sin in our humanity with the substantial Love-fire in his blood, he then ate the *Paschal Lamb* <note>The Passover lamb</note> with his Disciples. For he had given himself up into the Type through his Covenant of Grace, with the heavenly flesh, and intended to fulfill the Type with the flesh of Love. He would bring forth the Adamical humanity <note>The human nature inherited from Adam</note> through Death; namely: through God’s Anger, through the enclosure of the Adamical Life into God’s Love-fire, and break open the *Prison of Death*.

6. So now, in the Type of the *Paschal Lamb*, he began the New Testament—the *fulfillment* of these Types. He led his Disciples to the Sacrifice of the New Testament; namely: to the Altar of God, in his flesh and blood, so that they should eat the fulfillment of the Old Testament in the Sacrifice of the New Testament. For he brought the Old Testament—the *figure*—into the *New*, into his flesh and blood, which was the *Sacrifice of reconciliation for the sins of the World*. He gave them the <margin>Or, Reconciliation.</margin> atonement in his flesh to eat, and in his blood to drink.

7. For the reconciliation should no longer be performed in fire, where the Father’s anger burnt away the vanity in Man’s Faith; instead, it should be done in the Love-fire, in the *flesh of Christ*. They should now, with their faith's desire—the fiery life's mouth of the soul—eat and drink the reconciliation with Christ’s flesh and blood. *No longer* with Faith in the Type, but in Substance; no longer in power without Substance, but with substantial Divine and human power, wherein the *humanity of Christ himself* should be the Paschal Lamb: Not Deity without Humanity, but Deity and Humanity together.

8. For the *Paschal Lamb* must be eaten entirely, and so he would not give them only a piece of his humanity, but (Note) gave himself to them wholly and altogether, into their soul's fire-mouth—that is, into the desire of Faith. The soul’s fiery mouth was now the fire of God which devoured the Sacrifice, just as common fire devours oil and produces a clear light from its consuming. Thus also Christ gave to his Disciples—to their faith's desire, meaning the fiery mouth of the soul as the true Life—his heavenly Body and his heavenly Blood. He did this in the same manner and way as the heavenly *Ens* <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for "being" or "essence"</note> in the limit of the Covenant gave itself into *Mary*, into the vanished essence, and became one Person, wholly undivided.

9. Note: He gave his whole heavenly humanity to them into their Adamical humanity—understand this as the Paradisiacal Ground <note>The original, pure state of humanity as created in Paradise</note>. For every Life desires an *essence* of its own likeness; namely, every fire desires
